# Managing Your Subscription
Source: https://docs.gitar.ai/account-billing/managing-subscription

Upgrade or cancel a subscription, manage seats, and handle billing

## Upgrading Your Plan

Gitar offers a **14-day free trial** with full access to all Pro plan features. When you're ready to upgrade, visit your organization's billing settings.

> [card] [Upgrade Now](https://app.gitar.ai/upgrade?utm_source=docs\&utm_medium=website\&utm_campaign=docs_subscription\&utm_content=manage_subscription)
>
> Go to your billing settings to choose a plan and start your subscription.

For a full comparison of plan features and usage limits, see [Plans & Billing](/account-billing/plans).

## Managing Seats

> [!NOTE]
>
> Seat management applies to **Core** and **Pro**, where bots require their own paid seats. Enterprise plans include unlimited seats, so bots do not need separate seats.

Organization admins can add or remove seats from the billing settings page.

* **Adding a seat**: Assign a seat to a user from Settings → Billing. The user can trigger the features included in their plan.
* **Removing a seat**: Unassign a seat from a user. The user can still open PRs, but their events and commands no longer trigger features that require a seat.

> [card] [Manage Seats](https://app.gitar.ai/upgrade?utm_source=docs\&utm_medium=website\&utm_campaign=docs_subscription\&utm_content=manage_seats)
>
> Go to Settings → Billing to assign or remove seats.

For details on seat tiers and organization roles, see [Members & Seats](/administration/members).

## Switching Between Monthly and Annual Billing

Core and Pro plans support monthly or annual billing. See [Plans & Billing](/account-billing/plans) for pricing.

* **Monthly → Annual**: Takes effect immediately. You're charged a prorated amount for the remainder of the current period.
* **Annual → Monthly**: Takes effect at the end of your current annual period. You can cancel the switch any time before then from your billing settings.

## Canceling a subscription

Core and Pro subscriptions are canceled through Stripe's Billing Portal.

> [!WARNING]
>
> Disconnecting a repository does not cancel a subscription or stop billing. Complete the cancellation in Stripe's Billing Portal to stop future renewals.

1. Open the organization's **Settings → Billing** page.
2. Select **Manage Billing** to open Stripe's Billing Portal.
3. Select the subscription's cancellation option and complete the confirmation steps.
4. Check the portal for the cancellation status and the date the subscription ends.

Access continues until the end of the current billing period. The subscription will not renew after cancellation.

For Enterprise cancellation requests, or if the billing portal is unavailable, contact **[support@gitar.ai](mailto:support@gitar.ai)**.

## Billing & Invoices

Invoices and billing details are available in your organization's billing settings page. Billing is based on the number of seats assigned.

> [card] [View Billing](https://app.gitar.ai/upgrade?utm_source=docs\&utm_medium=website\&utm_campaign=docs_subscription\&utm_content=billing_settings)
>
> Go to your billing settings to view invoices and payment details.

> [!TIP]
>
> If you need a copy of a past invoice or have questions about a charge, contact **[support@gitar.ai](mailto:support@gitar.ai)**.

## Contacting Support

For any billing inquiry, contact **[support@gitar.ai](mailto:support@gitar.ai)**. Gitar typically responds within one business day.

## FAQ

> [accordion] How do I upgrade from the free trial?
>
> Visit your [billing settings](https://app.gitar.ai/upgrade?utm_source=docs\&utm_medium=website\&utm_campaign=docs_subscription\&utm_content=manage_subscription) and select a plan. Your trial features continue uninterrupted once you subscribe.

> [accordion] What happens when my trial ends?
>
> If the trial ends without a paid subscription, your organization moves to Free. PR summaries and Basic Insights remain available. Code review, including manual review commands, requires a plan that includes it.

> [accordion] Can I change my plan later?
>
> Yes. You can upgrade between Core and Pro at any time from your billing settings. To move to an Enterprise plan, contact **[support@gitar.ai](mailto:support@gitar.ai)**. Enterprise plans include unlimited seats, custom integrations, and dedicated support.

> [accordion] How do I cancel my subscription?
>
> Open **Settings → Billing → Manage Billing** and complete the cancellation in Stripe's Billing Portal. Disconnecting a repository does not cancel a subscription. See [Canceling a subscription](#canceling-a-subscription) for the steps and support options.


# Plans
Source: https://docs.gitar.ai/account-billing/plans

Plans and pricing details for Gitar

Gitar's paid plans are Core, Pro, and Enterprise. Eligible new organizations receive a **14-day free trial** of Pro. Vetted open-source projects can use Gitar for free on the [Open Source plan](#open-source-plan).

For current pricing, see [gitar.ai/pricing](https://gitar.ai/pricing).

> [!NOTE]
>
> PR summaries are free for unlimited public and private repos. No credit card required.

For details on managing seats and team members, see [Members & Seats](/administration/members).

## Feature Comparison

| Feature | Core | Pro | Enterprise |
| - | :-: | :-: | :-: |
| **Users** | Up to 50 | Up to 50 | Unlimited seats, including bots |
| **Repos** | Unlimited | Unlimited | Unlimited |
| Code reviews | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ |
| Thorough review | | | Included |
| [Agentic Security](/features/agentic-security) | | | Included |
| PR summaries | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ |
| CI failure analysis | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ |
| Fixes via comments | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ |
| Interactive agent | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ |
| Developer insights | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ |
| Auto-approve & merge blocking | | ✓ | ✓ |
| Auto-apply | | ✓ | ✓ |
| Advanced CI analysis | | ✓ | ✓ |
| Integrations (Slack, Linear, Jira) | | ✓ | ✓ |
| Custom checks & automations | | ✓ | ✓ |
| Advanced insights | | ✓ | ✓ |
| Custom integrations | | | ✓ |
| Functional validation | | | ✓ |
| [Knowledge](/features/knowledge) | | | Included |
| [Cross-Repo Analysis](/features/cross-repo-analysis) | | | Included |
| [Cross-PR Analysis](/features/cross-pr-analysis) | | | Included |
| API access | | | Available on request |
| Self-hosted GitLab | | | ✓ |
| Bring Your Own Model (Anthropic, AWS Bedrock, or your gateway) | | | Available on request |
| [SSO (OIDC or SAML)](/administration/sso) | | | ✓ |
| Audit logs | | | ✓ |
| Dedicated support | | | ✓ |
| Custom agreements | | | ✓ |

## Enterprise scale

Enterprise supports unlimited repository connections and custom workflows across the organization.

| Capability | Enterprise allowance |
| - | - |
| Review volume | Unlimited reviews per author, including [allowed bot authors](/configuration/settings#scope). |
| [Cross-Repo Analysis](/features/cross-repo-analysis) | Unlimited linked repositories, including across supported code hosts. |
| [Custom rules](/features/rules) | Unlimited custom rules per organization. |
| [Custom MCP integrations](/integrations/custom-integrations) | Unlimited MCP connections. |
| [Skills](/integrations/skills) | Unlimited reusable skills. |

Gitar supports [unlimited changed files per PR](/features/code-review#large-prs-and-review-volume), with context selected for the review.

## Usage Limits

Enterprise is exempt from the per-author full-review allowances and the cost-based processing budget described here. Core and Pro follow the usage policy below.

Full-review allowances are being measured during rollout. The existing budget
policy continues to apply. Enforced allowances will be published here when available.

When a full review is deferred, Gitar keeps existing findings visible and shows
when review capacity becomes available. Findings can still be rechecked. The
deferred report does not issue a new approval for the unreviewed commit.

## Open Source Plan

Gitar is free for vetted open-source projects. The Open Source plan includes everything in Pro, plus unlimited seats. Every contributor is covered, with no seats to assign.

### Eligibility

Projects are reviewed and approved by Gitar. To qualify:

* **An OSI-approved open-source license.** Applications are reviewed individually, and commercial open-source products generally don't qualify.
* **Public repositories only.** Private repos are never processed on this plan, even when they belong to the same organization.
* **Hosted on github.com or gitlab.com.** Self-hosted GitLab, Bitbucket, and Azure DevOps aren't available on this plan.

Each repository is approved individually. Repos that haven't been added to your account's allowlist will not be processed.

### Applying

Apply through the [Gitar for OSS application form](https://forms.gle/uESzuYr2Wzs9zm4L7), the same form linked from [gitar.ai](https://gitar.ai). You'll be asked for a contact email and links to the repositories you'd like covered.

Only apply if you are an owner or admin of those repositories, since installing the Gitar app requires it. Gitar reviews each application and follows up with next steps.

### Attribution

Gitar's comments on Open Source organizations include a short attribution identifying free open-source access. Other organizations do not show this line.

## Troubleshooting

> [accordion] Gitar Says I Don't Have a Seat
>
> Gitar posts that message when your organization is on a paid plan (Core or Pro) and no admin has allocated you a seat yet.
>
> * Ask a team admin to assign you a seat from Settings → Billing at [app.gitar.ai](https://app.gitar.ai)
> * During a trial period, seats are not enforced and all members have full access
> * See [Members & Seats](/administration/members) for more details


# Audit Logs
Source: https://docs.gitar.ai/administration/audit-logs

Track configuration changes and user activity in your organization

Audit logs record every configuration change made across your organization, with the user who made it and a timestamp.

> [!NOTE]
>
> Audit logs are available on the **Enterprise plan**.

## What Gets Logged

Gitar tracks the following types of events:

| Category | Examples |
| - | - |
| **Organization settings** | Enabling/disabling code review, changing auto-fix defaults |
| **Repository configuration** | Adding or updating repository-level settings |
| **Agent settings** | Modifications to agent behavior and rules |
| **Integrations** | Setup and removal of Jira, Slack, and Linear connections |
| **SSO configuration** | Changes to single sign-on settings |
| **Billing** | Checkout, plan changes, billing portal access, and seat updates |
| **Members and seats** | Seat assignments, role changes, and member management actions |

## Viewing Audit Logs

To access your audit logs:

1. Navigate to your organization settings in the [Gitar dashboard](https://app.gitar.ai).
2. Select **Audit Logs** from the sidebar.

Each log entry carries these fields:

| Field | What it holds |
| - | - |
| **Timestamp** | When the change occurred |
| **User** | Who made the change |
| **Action** | What was changed |
| **Details** | A summary of the action, such as the affected repository or setting |

## Browse and Export

Use the pagination controls to browse older entries. Select **Export CSV** to download the entries displayed on the current page, including their details and metadata.

## Retention

Audit logs are retained for the duration of your Enterprise subscription.


# Bring Your Own Model
Source: https://docs.gitar.ai/administration/bring-your-own-model

Serve Gitar's LLM calls from your own account, through Anthropic, AWS Bedrock, or your gateway

Gitar can run every LLM call for your organization on your account instead of Gitar's. Your provider agreement, your data boundary, your bill.

Gitar still chooses the model for each task. You choose the account those calls are served from, and where they run.

## Before you begin

* Bring Your Own Model must be enabled for your organization. [Contact support](mailto:support@gitar.ai?subject=Bring%20Your%20Own%20Model) if you do not see the card.
* You need the organization admin role.
* Open **Settings -> Configuration -> Bring Your Own Model**.

## Choose a provider

Pick one option from the provider list. One option serves traffic at a time.

| Option | What you supply |
| - | - |
| **Anthropic** | An API key |
| **AWS Bedrock** | A residency geography and an IAM role |
| **LiteLLM** | Your gateway URL and an API key |
| **Portkey** | A provider slug and an API key |
| **OpenRouter** | An API key |

## Set up Anthropic

Paste an API key. Gitar stores it encrypted and calls `api.anthropic.com` with it.

The Anthropic option with its API key field

The key needs access to Opus, Sonnet, and Haiku. Verification tells you if a workspace restriction is in the way.

## Set up AWS Bedrock

Gitar assumes a role in your account per request through AWS STS, so no standing credential of yours is stored with Gitar.

The AWS Bedrock option showing residency geography, role ARN, the global inference toggle, and the optional guardrail fields

**1. Pick your residency geography**

Gitar routes to a geographic inference profile, so requests stay inside that geography's regions on the AWS backbone.

| Geography | Regions AWS may route within |
| - | - |
| United States | us-east-1, us-east-2, us-west-2 |
| European Union | 6 EU regions, entering at eu-central-1 |
| Australia | ap-southeast-2 (Sydney), ap-southeast-4 (Melbourne) |
| Japan | ap-northeast-1 (Tokyo), ap-northeast-3 (Osaka) |

Your service control policies have to allow every region in the geography, because a request may land in any of them.

**2. Create the role in AWS**

Open **Create the role in AWS** in the card. It carries your external ID and the policies to copy, and it stays available afterwards for whenever you rebuild the role or audit who can assume it.

Name the role with the `gitar-bedrock-` prefix. Gitar's permission to assume roles is scoped to that prefix, so a role named anything else cannot be assumed however its trust policy reads.

The external ID is fixed for your organization and never changes. It is not a secret, but the role must require it. That condition is what stops anyone else who learns your role ARN from asking Gitar to assume it.

**3. Enable the models in your AWS account**

Submit the Anthropic use case details form, then have a principal with AWS Marketplace permissions invoke each model once from a region in the geography. That enables it account-wide.

Listing models is not the same as being allowed to call them, which is what verification checks.

**4. Paste the role ARN and verify**

Paste the ARN into the card, then select **Verify connection**.

### Global cross-region inference

Off by default. Global inference routes worldwide, including outside your geography, so turn it on only if throughput matters more than residency. With it off, a model your geography cannot serve fails rather than leaving the geography.

### Apply your own guardrail

Optional. Name a guardrail from your Bedrock account and Gitar sends it on every inference call it makes with your credentials, so your own PII and content policy runs on Gitar's traffic.

Bedrock guardrails are per-request. A guardrail configured in your account has no effect on anything until it is named here, which is why this field exists rather than being picked up automatically.

Enter the guardrail's ID or its full ARN, then a published version number or `DRAFT`. Both fields are required together, since Bedrock rejects an identifier without a version.

The role needs `bedrock:ApplyGuardrail` on the guardrail on top of the base permissions. **Create the role in AWS** shows that statement once you enter an identifier.

Verification sends its test calls with the guardrail attached, so a mistyped ID or a missing grant fails there rather than on your first review.

> [!WARNING]
>
> If you are using the guardrail to mask PII, set the input direction explicitly. Bedrock takes an action per direction, and a policy that sets only the default action leaves the input side untouched, so the model still receives the original text and only the response comes back masked. For masking before the model sees anything, set `inputAction` to `ANONYMIZE` and `inputEnabled` to `true` on each entity type.

> [!NOTE]
>
> A guardrail that masks code is a guardrail that changes what Gitar reviews. Placeholders like `{EMAIL}` replace the real text, so findings describe the masked version rather than your diff. Blocking is usually the better fit for source code, with masking reserved for the entity types you cannot let leave your account at all.

## Set up a gateway

LiteLLM, Portkey, and OpenRouter are configured the same way: the endpoint, and a credential for it. Gitar speaks the Anthropic Messages format to all three. You host the endpoint for LiteLLM, and Portkey and OpenRouter host theirs.

### LiteLLM

In **Gateway URL**, enter the `https://` origin your proxy serves on, with no path. In **LiteLLM API key**, enter a virtual key.

The LiteLLM option showing the gateway URL and API key fields

Gitar calls LiteLLM's [`/v1/messages` API](https://docs.litellm.ai/docs/anthropic_unified) using the Anthropic Messages format. LiteLLM looks up each requested `model_name` and routes it to the configured backend, including Anthropic, Bedrock, or Vertex.

The virtual key authenticates Gitar to LiteLLM. Keep backend credentials in LiteLLM and grant the virtual key access to the models shown by **Verify connection**. A Bedrock or Vertex deployment does not need a separate Anthropic API key.

| LiteLLM route | Model routing |
| - | - |
| `/v1/messages` | Gitar uses this route to invoke the gateway's configured `model_name` entries and their backends. |
| `/anthropic/v1/messages` | [Anthropic passthrough](https://docs.litellm.ai/docs/pass_through/anthropic_completion), with separate upstream Anthropic credentials. Gitar's LiteLLM option does not use this route. |

#### Troubleshoot refused requests

If every model shows **Refused**, check authentication before changing model names. A `401` with `API key is invalid` can come from the gateway or its upstream provider. The gateway logs identify which credential was rejected.

Test a small request to `/v1/messages` with the same virtual key. LiteLLM supports both `x-api-key: <virtual-key>` and `Authorization: Bearer <virtual-key>`. Gitar sends `x-api-key`.

If gateway logs show Gitar calling `/anthropic/v1/messages`, [contact support](mailto:support@gitar.ai?subject=LiteLLM%20Messages%20route). Older Gitar deployments selected that passthrough route. Changing a model alias does not fix a route authentication failure.

#### Gateways with model aliases

LiteLLM's `model_name` values are chosen by the gateway administrator. They are not a universal LiteLLM model catalog. Query `/v1/models` with the Gitar virtual key to find the names available to that key, then verify that those names can be invoked.

Gitar's Anthropic and LiteLLM options request `claude-haiku-4-5`, Anthropic's short alias for Haiku 4.5. The alias follows the most recent snapshot of that minor version. See Anthropic's [model IDs and versioning](https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/about-claude/models/model-ids-and-versions#before-the-4-6-generation).

For a model-name mismatch on `/v1/messages`, add a `model_list` entry with the exact name shown by **Verify connection**. Copy the existing deployment's `litellm_params` and change only `model_name`. Keep the original entry for other clients and grant the Gitar virtual key access to the added name.

For example, a deployment exposed only as `claude-haiku-4-5-20251001` can also be exposed as `claude-haiku-4-5`. Both entries must route to the intended Haiku model. These customer-configured aliases apply to native model routing, not Anthropic passthrough.

After the model names are configured, verify the connection and run a review to check streaming and tool calls. Include the LiteLLM version and upstream provider when contacting support.

#### Network access

Your proxy has to be reachable from the public internet. Gitar refuses a bare IP address, `localhost`, and any `.internal`, `.local`, or `.svc.cluster.local` host when you save, and refuses a hostname that resolves into private address space when it connects.

If your proxy enforces an IP allowlist, permit Gitar's public IP addresses. See [Network Access](/connecting-code/network-access).

### Portkey

Enter the provider slug you want the traffic routed through, then your Portkey API key. The slug is the name you gave the Anthropic integration in your Portkey workspace.

The Portkey option showing the provider slug and API key fields

> [!NOTE]
>
> A slug routes to whichever provider you wired up behind it, so the models Gitar needs have to be reachable through that provider. Verification calls each one and names any that are not.

### OpenRouter

Enter an API key.

The OpenRouter option with its API key field

OpenRouter is a broker rather than a boundary. If you are here for a data-residency requirement, Bedrock is the option that enforces one.

### Another gateway

For a gateway that is not listed, [contact support](mailto:support@gitar.ai?subject=BYO%20LLM%20gateway).

## Verify the connection

Every option is verified the same way, and saving stays disabled until verification passes. Gitar sends one small request per model it needs, from your account, and reports what came back for each.

| Row says | What it means |
| - | - |
| **Answered** | The model exists and this credential may call it |
| **Refused** | The endpoint denied the call. The credential may be invalid, upstream authentication may have failed, or model access may be restricted |
| **Not offered** | The endpoint does not carry this model: a geography without it, or a gateway routing to a catalog that lacks it |
| **No answer** | Throttled, or a transient fault. Nothing to change. Verify again |

## Monitor a live configuration

Gitar shows a card on your dashboard when inference detects a credential, billing, or configuration problem. Bedrock configurations also receive background connection checks. Reviews are held rather than quietly routed back through Gitar's account, so nothing leaves your boundary without you choosing it.

| Card | What to do |
| - | - |
| **API Key Invalid** | Your key was rejected. Re-enter it and verify again |
| **Permission Denied** | Your credential was refused. For Bedrock, check that the trust policy still names Gitar's principal and that model access is on |
| **Configuration Cannot Serve Traffic** | Your geography carries no model Gitar can use. Pick another geography, or allow global inference |
| **Spend Limit Reached** or **Billing Issue** | Raise the limit or settle the balance with your provider |

Saving a replacement key or changing the active provider configuration automatically resumes held work. A later successful provider request also resumes held work.

## What Gitar stores

An Anthropic or gateway credential is held encrypted in Gitar's secret store, and the configuration keeps only a reference to it.

A Bedrock configuration stores no credential at all. The role ARN, geography, and external ID are all non-secret, and the credential is minted per request by STS.

**Clear** removes the configuration and every credential stored with it, and returns your organization to Gitar's own account.


# Members & Seats
Source: https://docs.gitar.ai/administration/members

Manage organization members and billing seats

Gitar separates two things: **organization members**, the people with access to your Gitar dashboard, and **seats**, which control who Gitar reviews code for and what you're billed for. They're managed on different pages and don't have to match.

## Organization Members

Settings → Organization lists everyone with access to your Gitar dashboard, along with their role.

### Inviting Members

Organization admins invite members by email from Settings → Organization. Invited members receive an email to join, and appear as **Pending** until they accept.

### Roles and Permissions

| Role | Description |
| - | - |
| **Admin** | Can manage seats, configure settings, manage billing, and set up integrations. |
| **Member** | Can view the organization dashboard. |

> [!NOTE]
>
> Dashboard access and roles are unrelated to seats. An admin can have no seat, and a seated developer never needs dashboard access to have their PRs reviewed.

## Seat Management

> [!NOTE]
>
> Seat management applies to **Core** and **Pro**, where bots require their own paid seats. Enterprise plans include unlimited seats, so bots do not need separate seats.

Seats control who can trigger features that require one, including through PRs, pushes, and comments. Admins assign seats from Settings → Billing.

Unlike organization members, the seats list is pulled from your connected code hosting platform: it lists the members of your GitHub organization, GitLab group, Azure DevOps organization, or Bitbucket workspace, whether or not they've ever logged into Gitar. Admins can also add a user manually if your platform doesn't expose a member list Gitar can read.

| Platform | Flagged as external |
| - | - |
| **GitHub** | Outside collaborators |
| **GitLab** | Not flagged |
| **Azure DevOps** | Guest users |
| **Bitbucket** | Users with the `collaborator` permission |

### With a Seat

Users with an assigned seat get full access to Gitar's automatic capabilities, including:

* Automatic code review on their PRs
* CI failure analysis
* All other features included in their plan

### Without a Seat

Users without a seat can still open PRs, but Gitar does not automatically review their changes or act on their commands.

Gitar posts a notice when they open a PR and replies when they send a command. A seated teammate can still request work on the same PR.

No seat is added and nothing is billed until an admin assigns one from Settings → Billing.

### Seat Tiers

The seats page groups users into three groups:

| Group | Who is in it |
| - | - |
| **Seated** | Users with a seat assigned, receiving full Gitar features |
| **Active, no seat** | Users with no seat who have had Gitar activity in the last 30 days. Available for GitHub, Azure DevOps, and Bitbucket |
| **Inactive** | Users with no seat and no Gitar activity in the last 30 days. GitLab users appear here because activity status is unavailable |

### Trial Period

During your trial, seat limits are not enforced. Every PR author is reviewed regardless of seat assignment, so you can see Gitar in action across your whole team before deciding how many seats to buy.

## Billing Context

How seats relate to billing depends on your plan:

| Plan | Seats and billing |
| - | - |
| **Free** | PR summaries on unlimited repos. No seat management |
| **Core and Pro** | Usage is allocated per seat on a monthly billing cycle. You are billed on the number of seats assigned |
| **Enterprise** | Unlimited usage with no seat limits |

For full details on pricing and plan features, see [Plans & Billing](/account-billing/plans).

## Troubleshooting

> [accordion] A team member doesn't appear in the organization members list
>
> Members are invite-only. An admin needs to invite them by email from Settings → Organization.

> [accordion] A colleague isn't showing up on the seats page
>
> The seats page lists members of your connected GitHub organization, GitLab group, Azure DevOps organization, or Bitbucket workspace. Check that the person is a member there and, for Azure DevOps, that Gitar's token has the **Graph & Identity (Read)** scope. If your platform doesn't support a member list Gitar can read (for example, Azure DevOps Server), add the person manually from Settings → Billing.

> [accordion] Gitar says a user doesn't have a seat
>
> An admin needs to assign them a seat from Settings → Billing. During a trial period, seat limits are not enforced, so this message should not appear.


# Security
Source: https://docs.gitar.ai/administration/security

Security practices and data protection at Gitar

Gitar processes your code in ephemeral, isolated containers, encrypts it in transit and at rest, and never uses it to train models.

## Infrastructure Security

### Cloud Infrastructure

* **AWS-based Infrastructure**: All services run on Amazon Web Services with strict access controls
* **Multi-Environment Isolation**: Separate AWS accounts for development, staging, production, and management
* **Role-Based Access Control**: AWS SSO with AdministratorAccess roles limited to authorized personnel
* **Network Security**: Private VPCs with controlled ingress/egress rules

### Container Security

* **Non-Root Execution**: All containers run as non-privileged users
* **Minimal Attack Surface**: Containerized environments with only necessary dependencies
* **Image Security**: Regular security scanning of container images
* **Secure Entrypoints**: Custom bootstrap processes with security validations

## Authentication & Authorization

### Multi-Factor Authentication

* **Auth0 Integration**: Enterprise-grade authentication with MFA support
* **OAuth 2.0 Compliance**: Standards-compliant authentication flows
* **Session Management**: Secure session handling with automatic expiration

### Granular Permissions

* **Scope-Based Authorization**: Granular permissions
* **Organization Isolation**: Complete separation between different organizations
* **API Token Management**: Secure token generation with configurable expiration

## Data Protection

### Encryption

* **Data in Transit**: All API communications encrypted with TLS 1.3
* **Data at Rest**: AES-256-GCM encryption for sensitive file storage
* **Key Management**: SHA-256 key derivation with proper nonce generation
* **Secure Random Generation**: Cryptographically secure random number generation

### Code Repository Security

* **Secure Checkout**: Repository validation before any code access
* **Credential Management**: Secure Git credential handling for GitHub and GitLab
* **Workspace Isolation**: Sandboxed execution environments for code operations
* **Path Sanitization**: Proper validation of file paths and repository structures

## Secret Management

### AWS Secrets Manager Integration

* **Centralized Secret Storage**: All sensitive credentials stored in AWS Secrets Manager
* **Environment-Specific Secrets**: Separate secret management for dev, staging, and production
* **Automatic Rotation**: Support for automatic secret rotation

### Service Credentials

* **Database Security**: Encrypted connection strings and credentials
* **Third-Party Integrations**: Secure storage of GitHub, GitLab, Slack, and other API tokens
* **Token Refresh**: Automatic handling of token expiration and renewal

## Data Retention & Privacy

### Zero Data Retention Policy

* **Temporary Processing Only**: All code processing occurs in temporary, isolated containers
* **No Code Storage**: No source code retained beyond what's necessary to provide functionality and performance
* **Disk Caching for Performance**: Code cached on file volumes for limited durations to improve latency
* **Encrypted RAM**: Memory encryption for sensitive processing operations
* **Third-Party Agreements**: Zero data retention agreements with all AI providers, listed on the [Sonar sub-processor list](https://www.sonarsource.com/legal/sub-processors/)

### Code Access Policy

* **Minimal Code Access**: Only necessary code portions accessed for maintenance tasks
* **Temporary Processing**: Code processed in secure, ephemeral environments
* **Audit Trail**: Complete logging of all code access and operations

## Webhook Security

### Signature Verification

* **HMAC-SHA256 Validation**: All webhooks verified using HMAC-SHA256 signatures
* **Constant-Time Comparison**: Cryptographically secure signature comparison
* **Timestamp Validation**: Configurable tolerance (default 5 minutes) to prevent replay attacks
* **Multiple Provider Support**: Dedicated validation for GitHub, GitLab, Slack, Linear, and other providers

## Secure Development Lifecycle (SDLC)

### Development Practices

* **Security by Design**: Security considerations integrated from initial design phase
* **Code Review Requirements**: All code changes require security-focused peer review
* **Dependency Scanning**: Continuous monitoring of third-party dependencies for vulnerabilities

### Security Testing

* **Penetration Testing**: Annual third-party penetration tests
* **Vulnerability Assessments**: Quarterly security assessments

### Deployment Security

* **Infrastructure as Code**: All infrastructure changes version controlled and reviewed
* **Immutable Infrastructure**: Production deployments use immutable container images
* **Environment Segregation**: Strict separation between dev, staging, and production

### Security Training

* **Annual Security Training**: Mandatory security awareness training for all engineers
* **Incident Response Drills**: Quarterly security incident simulations

## Data Storage & Processing

### Storage Architecture

* **No Local Storage**: Code processing servers have no persistent local storage
* **Ephemeral Operations**: Code analysis performed entirely in temporary storage
* **Stateless Processing**: Each task runs in completely stateless environment

### Processing Isolation

* **Container Isolation**: Each customer task runs in separate container
* **Network Isolation**: No network access between customer processing environments
* **Resource Limits**: Strict CPU and memory limits per processing task
* **Time Limits**: Maximum execution time enforced for all operations

### Data Flow Security

* **End-to-End Encryption**: Code encrypted from repository to processing to disposal
* **No Intermediate Storage**: Direct streaming from source to processing
* **Audit Trail**: Complete audit log of data access without storing actual content

## Audit & Compliance

### Logging

* **Action Tracking**: All user actions logged with complete metadata
* **Audit Trail**: Immutable audit logs for compliance and security analysis

### Compliance Framework

* **SOC 2 and ISO 27001**: Security controls aligned with SOC 2 Type II and ISO 27001
* **GDPR Compliance**: Data protection practices compliant with GDPR requirements
* **Regular Assessments**: Ongoing security assessments and improvements

## Integration Security

### Third-Party Integrations

* **OAuth 2.0 Implementation**: Standards-compliant OAuth flows for all integrations
* **State Parameter Validation**: CSRF protection through state parameter verification
* **Scope Limitation**: Minimal necessary permissions requested from integrated services
* **Secure Uninstallation**: Proper cleanup of credentials and data upon integration removal

### API Security

* **Rate Limiting**: Protection against abuse through configurable rate limits
* **Input Validation**: Every API input validated before use
* **Error Handling**: Secure error responses without information leakage
* **CORS Configuration**: Properly configured cross-origin resource sharing

## Vulnerability Management

### Security Monitoring

* **Dependency Management**: Regular updates and security patches for all dependencies

### Incident Response

* **Response Plan**: Documented incident response procedures
* **Communication Protocol**: Clear communication channels for security incidents
* **Recovery Procedures**: Tested backup and recovery processes

## Enterprise Features

### Advanced Security Controls

* **IP Allowlisting**: Restrict access to specific IP ranges
* **SAML SSO**: Enterprise single sign-on with SAML 2.0
* **Okta Integration**: Native integration with Okta identity provider

## Frequently Asked Questions

> [accordion] How does Gitar access my code repositories?
>
> Gitar accesses repositories through secure OAuth integrations with GitHub and GitLab. We only access the specific repositories you configure and only perform the maintenance tasks you approve. All repository access is logged and auditable.

> [accordion] Is my code stored on Gitar's servers?
>
> No, we maintain a strict zero data retention policy. Code is processed exclusively in Gitar-managed ephemeral environments that are destroyed immediately after task completion. We have zero data retention agreements with all our AI providers, ensuring your code is never stored or used for training.

> [accordion] Does Gitar train AI models on my code?
>
> No, Gitar does not use customer code for training AI models. Your code remains private and is only used to perform the specific maintenance tasks you request.

> [accordion] How does Gitar handle secrets in my code?
>
> Gitar's processing environments are designed to avoid exposing secrets. Any secrets encountered during processing are not logged, stored, or transmitted outside the secure processing environment. Our ephemeral architecture ensures secrets exist only in encrypted memory during processing and are erased upon completion.

> [accordion] What certifications does Gitar have?
>
> We maintain security practices aligned with SOC 2 Type II / ISO 27001 requirements and are fully certified. We conduct regular third-party security assessments and maintain compliance with industry standards.

> [accordion] What are your zero data retention agreements?
>
> We maintain contractual zero data retention agreements with all AI providers. The current providers are on the [Sonar sub-processor list](https://www.sonarsource.com/legal/sub-processors/). These agreements ensure that no customer code is retained, stored, or used for model training. All processing occurs with zero-retention API endpoints.

> [accordion] How does Gitar isolate different organizations?
>
> Organizations are isolated at the database, application, and infrastructure levels. No organization can access another's data, configurations, or processing environments.

## Contact & Support

For security-related questions or to report security vulnerabilities:

* **Security Team**: [security@gitar.ai](mailto:security@gitar.ai)
* **General Support**: [developers@gitar.ai](mailto:developers@gitar.ai)


# SSO
Source: https://docs.gitar.ai/administration/sso

Use SSO to log in to the Gitar App

Single Sign-On (SSO) lets your team sign in to Gitar with the identity provider they already use, rather than a separate set of Gitar credentials.

> [!NOTE]
>
> SSO is available on the Enterprise plan. Contact [developers@gitar.ai](mailto:developers@gitar.ai) to enable SSO for your organization.

## Choose a Protocol

Gitar supports OpenID Connect (OIDC) and SAML 2.0. We recommend OIDC, as it needs fewer values to exchange and has no certificates to rotate. Use SAML if your identity provider or security policy requires it.

Okta is documented step by step below for both protocols. Other providers, including Microsoft Entra ID, Google Workspace, and OneLogin, follow the generic OIDC or SAML steps.

## Send Credentials Securely

> [!WARNING]
>
> Never send a client secret by email, chat, or support ticket. Share it through 1Password or another secure secret-sharing tool your security team approves. Gitar will confirm the method with you before setup.

Client IDs, domains, SAML sign-in URLs, and SAML signing certificates are not secret and can be sent by email.

## How Users Sign In

Users open Gitar from a tile on their identity provider's app dashboard, for example in Okta. The tile opens a Gitar login link that Gitar sends you during setup. Starting SSO sign-in from the Gitar login page is not supported yet.

## Okta Setup (OIDC)

**1. Request SSO Configuration**

Contact your Gitar account manager or email [developers@gitar.ai](mailto:developers@gitar.ai) to request OIDC SSO. Include the email domains your team signs in with, for example `example.com`.

**2. Create Okta Application**

In your Okta admin console, go to **Applications > Applications** and click **Create App Integration**. Select **OIDC - OpenID Connect** as the sign-in method and **Web Application** as the application type.

**3. Configure the Application**

Name the application "Gitar" and optionally use the Gitar logo at `/assets/images/gitar-brand-logo-dark.png`. Configure the following settings:

| Setting | Value |
| - | - |
| Grant type | Authorization Code |
| Sign-in redirect URI | `https://auth.sonarqube.us/login/callback` |
| Controlled access | Limit access to the groups that need Gitar |

Save the application.

**4. Send Configuration to Gitar**

Send the following values to your Gitar account manager:

| Value | Where to find it | Secret |
| - | - | - |
| Okta domain | Your Okta org URL, for example `example.okta.com` | No |
| Client ID | The **General** tab of the Gitar application | No |
| Client Secret | The **General** tab of the Gitar application | Yes |

Send the Client Secret through a [secure channel](#send-credentials-securely). Gitar will reply with your **Initiate login URI**.

**5. Configure Login**

On the application's **General** tab, click **Edit** under **Login** and set:

| Setting | Value |
| - | - |
| Login initiated by | Either Okta or App |
| Application visibility | Display application icon to users |
| Login flow | Redirect to app to initiate login (OIDC Compliant) |
| Initiate login URI | The **Initiate login URI** from Gitar |

The Initiate login URI has the form `https://app.gitar.ai/auth/login?connection=<connection>&organization=<organization>`.

**6. Assign Users**

Assign the appropriate users and groups to the Gitar application in Okta. Gitar will confirm once the connection is active.

## Okta Setup (SAML)

**1. Request SSO Configuration**

Contact your Gitar account manager or email [developers@gitar.ai](mailto:developers@gitar.ai) to request SAML SSO. Include the email domains your team signs in with. You will receive an email with your **Single sign-on URL** and **Audience URI**, which are required to set up the Okta integration.

**2. Create Okta Application**

In your Okta admin console, go to **Applications > Applications** and click **Create App Integration**. Select **SAML 2.0**. Name the application "Gitar" and optionally use the Gitar logo at `/assets/images/gitar-brand-logo-dark.png`.

Check **Do not display application icon to users**. Users sign in through a bookmark tile you add in a later step.

![Okta SAML setup](https://mintcdn.com/gitar/IlYvr_ecMR0aE0ph/assets/images/okta-saml-1.png?fit=max\&auto=format\&n=IlYvr_ecMR0aE0ph\&q=85\&s=10310f66653734d90c422959741023d2)

**3. Configure SAML Settings**

Enter the values from the email you received from Gitar:

| Okta setting | Value |
| - | - |
| Single sign-on URL | The **Single sign-on URL** from Gitar |
| Audience URI (SP Entity ID) | The **Audience URI** from Gitar |
| Name ID format | EmailAddress |
| Application username | Email |

Keep **Use this for Recipient URL and Destination URL** checked.

**4. Set Attribute Mappings**

Under **Attribute statements**, click **Show legacy configuration**, then click **Edit** next to **Profile attribute statements** and add the following attributes:

| Name | Value |
| - | - |
| `email` | `user.email` |
| `name` | `user.displayName` |

![Attribute mappings](https://mintcdn.com/gitar/lQ7K0XbiEqwSfNkm/assets/images/okta-saml-attributes.png?fit=max\&auto=format\&n=lQ7K0XbiEqwSfNkm\&q=85\&s=e3f963bf7b716d138485d262831029b3)

**5. Send Configuration to Gitar**

Assign the appropriate users and groups to the Gitar application in Okta. Then open the application's **Sign On** tab and click **More details** under **SAML 2.0**. Send the following to your Gitar account manager:

| Value | Example |
| - | - |
| Sign on URL | `https://example.okta.com/app/example_gitar_1/exk1a2b3c4/sso/saml` |
| Signing Certificate | The certificate file from **Download** |

Gitar will reply with your **Gitar login link**.

**6. Add a Bookmark App**

Go to **Applications > Applications**, click **Browse App Catalog**, and add the **Bookmark App** integration. Name it "Gitar" and set the URL to your **Gitar login link**.

The link has the form `https://app.gitar.ai/auth/login?connection=<connection>&organization=<organization>`. Assign the bookmark to the same users and groups as the SAML application.

## Generic OIDC Provider

1. Contact [developers@gitar.ai](mailto:developers@gitar.ai) to request OIDC SSO, and include the email domains your team signs in with.
2. Create a web application in your identity provider using the Authorization Code flow.
3. Set the redirect URI to `https://auth.sonarqube.us/login/callback`.
4. Allow the `openid`, `profile`, and `email` scopes.
5. Send Gitar the **Issuer URL**, **Client ID**, and **Client Secret**. Send the Client Secret through a [secure channel](#send-credentials-securely).
6. Set the application's initiate login URI, sometimes called the login or launch URL, to the **Initiate login URI** Gitar sends you.

The Issuer URL is the base URL that serves `/.well-known/openid-configuration`, for example `https://login.example.com`.

## Generic SAML 2.0 Provider

1. Contact [developers@gitar.ai](mailto:developers@gitar.ai) to request SAML SSO, and include the email domains your team signs in with. Gitar will send you an **Assertion Consumer Service (ACS) URL** and an **Entity ID**.
2. Create a new **SAML 2.0** application in your identity provider. Use the ACS URL as the single sign-on or reply URL, and the Entity ID as the audience or identifier.
3. Set the Name ID format to email address.
4. Map an `email` attribute to the user's email address and a `name` attribute to the user's display name.
5. Send Gitar the **Sign-in URL**, sometimes called the SSO URL or login URL, and the **X.509 signing certificate** from your identity provider.
6. Gitar will reply with your **Gitar login link**. Add it to your users' app dashboard as a bookmark or link tile, and hide the SAML application's own tile.

## Troubleshooting

> [accordion] Users can't sign in with SSO
>
> Verify that the affected users are assigned to the Gitar application in your identity provider. For SAML, check that the `email` and `name` attributes are mapped. Users must start sign-in from the Gitar tile on your identity provider's app dashboard, not the Gitar login page. For SAML, that is the bookmark tile, not the SAML application's tile.

> [accordion] Redirect URI mismatch error
>
> For OIDC, check that the sign-in redirect URI in your identity provider is exactly `https://auth.sonarqube.us/login/callback`. For SAML, check that the Single sign-on URL matches exactly what Gitar sent you. Even small differences such as a trailing slash can cause redirect errors.

> [accordion] How do I update my SSO configuration?
>
> Contact [developers@gitar.ai](mailto:developers@gitar.ai) to update your SSO settings or rotate credentials. Send any new client secret through a [secure channel](#send-credentials-securely). For SAML, send Gitar the new signing certificate before the old one expires.


# Get Gitar's blocking status for a GitLab MR
Source: https://docs.gitar.ai/api-reference/gitlab/get-mr-status

get /external/gitlab/mr_status
Returns the current Gitar blocking status for a GitLab merge request. Intended for use in CI pipelines: fail the job when `blocked == "yes"` and mark it required for merge.

Organizations with a single connected GitLab instance can omit `host`. On a multi-instance organization, pass `host` to say which instance the project id belongs to — GitLab project ids are only unique within one instance.

## Overview

The endpoint reports whether Gitar is blocking a GitLab merge request.

Point a CI job at it and fail the job when `blocked` is `"yes"`. Marking that job as required for merge in your GitLab project settings gives you merge blocking without GitLab approval rule API access.

## Prerequisites

* The GitLab project must be connected to Gitar. See [Connect GitLab](/connecting-code/gitlab).
* You need a Gitar API token with `integrations:read` scope. Store it as a CI/CD variable, for example `GITAR_TOKEN`, in your GitLab project settings.

## Setting Up the CI Job

Add the following job to your `.gitlab-ci.yml`:

```yaml theme={null}
gitar-check:
  stage: test
  script:
    - |
      BLOCKED=$(curl -sf -H "Authorization: Bearer $GITAR_TOKEN" \
        "https://api.gitar.ai/v1/external/gitlab/mr_status?project_id=$CI_PROJECT_ID&mr_iid=$CI_MERGE_REQUEST_IID" \
        | python3 -c "import sys,json; print(json.load(sys.stdin)['blocked'])")
      echo "Gitar blocking status: $BLOCKED"
      if [ "$BLOCKED" = "yes" ]; then
        echo "This MR is blocked by Gitar. Resolve the findings or comment 'gitar unblock' to bypass."
        exit 1
      fi
  rules:
    - if: $CI_PIPELINE_SOURCE == "merge_request_event"
```

> [!TIP]
>
> `CI_PROJECT_ID`, `CI_MERGE_REQUEST_IID`, and `CI_PIPELINE_SOURCE` are predefined GitLab CI/CD variables automatically available in every pipeline. You do not need to set these yourself.

Once the job is added, turn on **Settings → Merge requests → Merge checks → Pipelines must succeed** in your GitLab project. A failing `gitar-check` job fails the pipeline, and GitLab then refuses the merge. This works on every GitLab tier, which is the point. Gitar blocks through merge-request approval rules by default, and those are a Premium and Ultimate feature.

> [!NOTE]
>
> "Pipelines must succeed" gates on the whole pipeline, not on this job alone, so a pipeline that fails for an unrelated reason also blocks the merge. If you want a gate scoped to Gitar alone and you are on Premium or Ultimate, Gitar's own approval rule already gives you that and you do not need this job.

## Multiple GitLab Instances

If your organization has connected more than one GitLab instance, add `&host=$CI_SERVER_URL` to the request. GitLab project IDs are only unique within a single instance. Without a host, Gitar can match a same-numbered project on your other instance and report `pending` for a merge request that is blocked.

```text theme={null}
"https://api.gitar.ai/v1/external/gitlab/mr_status?project_id=$CI_PROJECT_ID&mr_iid=$CI_MERGE_REQUEST_IID&host=$CI_SERVER_URL"
```

`CI_SERVER_URL` is a predefined GitLab CI/CD variable holding the URL of the instance running the pipeline, so the same job definition works on every instance. Organizations with a single connected instance can omit it.

## Blocking Status Values

| Value | Meaning |
| - | - |
| `yes` | Gitar found issues. The CI job fails and the MR cannot be merged. |
| `no` | Gitar approved the MR, or a human bypassed the block. The CI job passes. |
| `pending` | Gitar has not reviewed this MR yet. The CI job passes by default. |

## Bypassing a Block

If a merge request is blocked but needs to be merged urgently, a team member can comment `gitar unblock` on the MR. Gitar processes the command, updates the status, and re-triggers the pipeline. The `gitar-check` job picks up the new status and passes, and the MR can merge.


# Onboard a GitLab project
Source: https://docs.gitar.ai/api-reference/gitlab/onboard-project

post /external/gitlab/projects
Add a GitLab project to Gitar and configure webhooks for code review. This endpoint is idempotent — calling it for an already-connected project returns success with status `already_connected`.

**Selecting a GitLab instance.** Organizations with a single connected GitLab instance can omit `host`. Once an organization connects more than one instance, `host` becomes required: without it Gitar cannot tell which instance a project id or path refers to, and the request is rejected with `409 Conflict` listing the connected instances.

## Prerequisites

The API token needs `integrations:write` scope.

The connected GitLab account must be a **Maintainer** or higher on the project you want to onboard. Without that access the request fails. See [Connect GitLab](/connecting-code/gitlab) for setup instructions.

## Project Identification

Identify the project by numeric ID or by path. If both are provided, `project_id` takes precedence.

| Field | Type | Example |
| - | - | - |
| `project_id` | integer | `12345` |
| `project_path` | string | `"my-group/my-project"` |

> [!TIP]
>
> A project's numeric ID appears on its GitLab settings page, and in the GitLab API response for `GET /api/v4/projects/:id`.

## Multiple GitLab Instances

If your organization has connected a single GitLab instance, omit `host` and Gitar uses it.

Once your organization connects more than one instance, `host` becomes **required**. GitLab project IDs and paths are only unique within one instance, so without a host Gitar cannot tell which instance you mean. A request that omits `host`, or names a host you have not connected, is rejected with `409 Conflict` and the response lists your connected instances.

```json theme={null}
{
  "project_path": "my-group/my-project",
  "host": "https://gitlab.com"
}
```

> [!NOTE]
>
> Connecting the **same project** from two instances is supported. GitLab assigns it a separate ID on each, so Gitar tracks them separately. This is the normal case while migrating between instances.
>
> Two **different** projects that happen to share a numeric ID across two of your instances are not supported. Onboarding the second one returns `409 Conflict` naming the instance that already holds the ID.

## Idempotent Behavior

Calling this endpoint repeatedly for the same project is safe. If the project is already connected to Gitar, the response returns `"status": "already_connected"` rather than creating a duplicate.

## What This Endpoint Does

When you onboard a project, Gitar:

1. **Verifies** the project exists in the GitLab instance you selected
2. **Registers** the project for Gitar code review
3. **Configures** a project-level webhook so Gitar receives push and merge request events


# Get installation health
Source: https://docs.gitar.ai/api-reference/installation/get-installation-health

get /installation/health

Check connected integration health with this request URL:

```bash theme={null}
curl https://api.gitar.ai/v1/external/installation/health \
  -H "Authorization: Bearer $GITAR_API_TOKEN"
```

The token needs `integrations:read` scope. The response includes connected integrations only.


# Create a partner organization
Source: https://docs.gitar.ai/api-reference/organizations/create-organization

post /external/organizations
Requires the organizations:create scope (or an administrator token) and the CreateOrganizations entitlement. Creates an Auth0 organization and emails the recipient an invitation to join it as admin. Parenting records attribution and grants no access.

Each call creates a new organization, including retries after a partial failure. A partner may create a limited number of organizations per rolling 24 hours (500 by default) and receives 429 beyond that. An optional API offer is recorded at signup and the trial starts on the first code connection. Retired or repriced offers fall back to the standard trial at activation.

## Prerequisites

The creating organization needs the `CreateOrganizations` entitlement. The API token needs `organizations:create` or `admin` scope. API token management also requires `GitarAPI` access. See [Creating an API Token](/api-reference/overview#creating-an-api-token).

## Signup offers

Use the offer code supplied for the partner integration, or omit `offer_code` for the standard trial. The trial starts when the new organization connects its first code host. Public signup offer codes are not accepted by this endpoint.

## Repeated requests

Each successful request creates a separate organization, even when the email already belongs to a user. Retrying after a timeout or partial failure can create another organization.

The recipient receives an email invitation to join the new organization as an administrator. The invitation expires after 30 days. The parent relationship records who created it and grants no access to the creating partner.

## Rate limit

A partner can create 500 organizations per rolling 24 hours by default. Further requests return `429` and create nothing. Contact Gitar to raise the limit.


# Overview
Source: https://docs.gitar.ai/api-reference/overview

Learn how to authenticate and use the Gitar API to manage your installation

## Base URL

```text theme={null}
https://api.gitar.ai/v1
```

## Authentication

Every endpoint requires a bearer token in the `Authorization` header:

```text theme={null}
Authorization: Bearer <GITAR_API_TOKEN>
```

## Creating an API Token

**1. Navigate to Organization Settings**

Go to [app.gitar.ai](https://app.gitar.ai) and open **Settings → API Tokens**.

**2. Create New Token**

Select **Create Token** on the API Tokens page.

**3. Configure Token**

Set the following options:

* **Alias** (optional): Descriptive name, for example `Health Monitor`
* **Duration**: Token expiration (default: 30 days)

**4. Copy and Store Securely**

Copy your token immediately. It is not shown again. Store it securely in your CI/CD secrets or environment variables.

## Quick Example: Check Installation Health

```bash cURL theme={null}
curl https://api.gitar.ai/v1/external/installation/health \
  -H "Authorization: Bearer $GITAR_API_TOKEN"
```

```python Python theme={null}
import requests
import os

response = requests.get(
    "https://api.gitar.ai/v1/external/installation/health",
    headers={
        "Authorization": f"Bearer {os.environ['GITAR_API_TOKEN']}"
    }
)

data = response.json()
for platform, info in data["code_hosting"].items():
    print(f"{platform}: {info['status']}")
```

```typescript TypeScript theme={null}
const response = await fetch("https://api.gitar.ai/v1/external/installation/health", {
  headers: {
    "Authorization": `Bearer ${process.env.GITAR_API_TOKEN}`
  }
});

const { code_hosting } = await response.json();
if (code_hosting.gitlab) console.log(`GitLab: ${code_hosting.gitlab.status}`);
if (code_hosting.github) console.log(`GitHub: ${code_hosting.github.status}`);
```

## Response Format

All API responses are JSON. Successful responses include the requested data:

```json theme={null}
{
  "code_hosting": {
    "gitlab": {
      "status": "ok",
      "host": "gitlab.example.com",
      "group": "my-org"
    },
    "github": {
      "status": "ok"
    }
  }
}
```

Error responses include a message:

```json theme={null}
{
  "message": "Not Found"
}
```

## Next Steps

> [card] [Create a partner organization](/api-reference/organizations/create-organization)
>
> Create a customer organization with an optional signup offer.

> [card] [Onboard GitLab Project](/api-reference/gitlab/onboard-project)
>
> Add a GitLab project to Gitar for code review

> [card] [Installation Health](/api-reference/installation/get-installation-health)
>
> Check the health status of your code hosting installation


# Commands & Interactions
Source: https://docs.gitar.ai/commands

Control Gitar's behavior through PR comments and commands

## Interacting with Gitar

You can interact with Gitar in PR and MR comments. Start your comment with `gitar` or `@gitar` (case-insensitive), mention `@gitar-bot` anywhere in the comment (GitHub), or reply directly to one of Gitar's comments or inline findings (no prefix needed). On GitLab, you can also mention the connected GitLab account, for example `@gitar-service-yourorg`.

On Azure DevOps Server, follow the [comment checkbox instructions](/connecting-code/azure-devops-server#comments-and-commands) if the PR description includes **Ask Gitar to read my new comments**.

Commands need write access to the PR, and bot accounts are ignored unless an admin lists them under **Allowed bots** in [scope settings](/configuration/settings#scope).

Gitar understands natural language. Ask it to fix something, make changes, or answer questions:

* `gitar fix this` or reply `fix` on an inline finding
* `gitar please add a unit test for this`
* `gitar why was this file changed?`
* `gitar create a Jira ticket for this`

Where supported, Gitar reacts with eyes when it starts processing and thumbs-up when a command is processed or an integration request completes. Azure DevOps and both Bitbucket editions do not show these reactions.

## Commands

Commands are exact phrases that control Gitar's behavior on a specific PR. They must match exactly (case-insensitive). Include multiple commands on separate lines. If two conflict, the last one wins.

| Command | Syntax |
| - | - |
| Unblock | `gitar unblock` |
| Auto-apply on | `gitar auto-apply:on` |
| Auto-apply off | `gitar auto-apply:off` |
| Auto-merge on | `gitar auto-merge:on` |
| Auto-merge off | `gitar auto-merge:off` |
| Display verbose | `gitar display:verbose` |
| Display compact | `gitar display:compact` |

***

### Unblock

Comment `gitar unblock` to dismiss Gitar's blocking review so you can merge immediately. See [Block Merge](/features/code-review/block-merge) for details on how merge blocking works.

gitar unblock command dismissing a blocking review

> [!NOTE]
>
> The `gitar unblock` command must be enabled by an org admin in **Settings -> Configuration -> Behavior**. The hint
> in review comments only appears when Gitar is actually blocking the PR. The command is not available on
> Bitbucket Data Center, where Gitar's review does not gate the merge.

Trailing punctuation is accepted. Both `gitar unblock.` and `gitar unblock!` work.

***

### Auto-Apply

Control whether Gitar automatically commits fixes to your branch for code review findings and CI failures.

| Command | Description |
| - | - |
| `gitar auto-apply:on` | Enable automatic fix application |
| `gitar auto-apply:off` | Disable automatic fixes (analysis only) |

The preference lasts for the life of the PR. The dashboard comment shows the auto-apply status. [Auto-Apply](/features/code-review/auto-apply) explains how organization and repository criteria limit which review findings Gitar may fix.

Auto-apply status in dashboard comment

Using gitar auto-apply command

***

### Auto-Apply by label

On GitHub and GitLab, [`gitar-managed`](/labels#gitar-managed) enables auto-apply for a PR. See [Labels](/labels#settings-and-removal) for setting precedence and removal behavior.

***

### Auto-Merge

Override your organization's auto-merge setting for one PR.

| Command | Description |
| - | - |
| `gitar auto-merge:on` | Arm auto-merge on this PR |
| `gitar auto-merge:off` | Leave this PR for a human to merge |

The command decides this PR only and holds for the life of it. See [Auto-Merge](/features/code-review/auto-merge) for what has to be true before an armed PR actually merges.

***

### Display Mode

Control how much detail you see in the Gitar dashboard comment. The default is `compact` mode.

| Command | Description |
| - | - |
| `gitar display:verbose` | Show all rules including those that don't apply, with explanations |
| `gitar display:compact` | Show only relevant rules (default) |

Your display preference persists across comment updates. The footer shows a hint for the opposite command.

**Compact mode** (default) shows only applicable rules with a summary of how many were hidden:

Compact display mode

**Verbose mode** shows all rules with explanations for why each rule doesn't apply:

Verbose display mode


# Configuration Repository
Source: https://docs.gitar.ai/configuration/configuration-repository

Share configuration from up to three repositories across your organization

Select up to three configuration repositories. Every repository in the organization inherits their shared configuration.

Gitar reads shared configuration and AI instruction files from each selected repository's default branch alongside the repository under review's own configuration. Teams can keep their shared files in separate repositories.

## Before you begin

* The configuration repository is available on the **Pro** and **Enterprise** plans. It is being rolled out per organization, so [contact support](mailto:support@gitar.ai?subject=Configuration%20repository) if you do not see the picker.
* You need the organization admin role.
* The repository has to be connected to Gitar already. AWS CodeCommit repositories are not selectable.

## What it holds

Shared configuration includes these paths:

| Directory | What it does | Override |
| - | - | - |
| `.gitar/skills/` | [Skills](/integrations/skills) every repository's agents can call | A repository's own skill of the same `name` wins |
| `.gitar/rules/` | [Rules](/features/rules) every repository evaluates | A repository's own rule file of the same name wins (case and `-`/`_` insensitive) |
| `.gitar/review/` | [Review instructions](/configuration/repository-config#custom-review-instructions) applied to every review | Both load, the organization's first |
| `.gitar/instructions/` | Instructions for every Gitar agent | Both load |
| [AI instruction files](/configuration/repository-config#ai-instruction-files), including `CLAUDE.md`, `AGENTS.md` and `.claude/rules/` | Shared conventions for code review and CI failure fixing | Both load, shared instructions first |
| `.gitar/cross-repo.md` | [Cross-repo guidance](/features/cross-repo-analysis) naming dependencies to include or exclude | Both load |
| `.gitar/config/approve.md`, `.gitar/config/merge.md`, `.gitar/config/risk.md` | Shared auto-approve, auto-merge, and risk criteria that extend the organization baseline | Both apply, the repository's own on top. `apply.md` is not shared |

`.gitar/config/apply.md` remains per repository and is not inherited.

Local rules and skills take precedence. Among shared repositories, the first selected repository wins a duplicate rule or skill. The settings list shows that order.

Instruction files combine guidance from every selected repository, followed by local instructions. They share the existing instruction budget, so adding repositories does not multiply that budget. A local instruction file does not replace shared instructions.

All selected repositories apply organization-wide. GitLab group assignments are not supported.

## Set the configuration repository

**1. Create or pick a repository**

Any connected repository works. A repository named `.gitar` is a clear place for it.

**2. Commit your shared configuration to it**

The same paths a repository uses for its own:

```text theme={null}
.gitar/
  skills/
    migration-check/
      SKILL.md
  rules/
    no-raw-sql.md
  review/
    gotchas.md
  config/
    approve.md
    merge.md
    risk.md
  instructions/
    conventions.md
  cross-repo.md
```

Add whichever you need. See [Writing a skill](/integrations/skills#writing-a-skill), [Rules](/features/rules) and [Custom review instructions](/configuration/repository-config#custom-review-instructions) for each file format. Push to the default branch.

**3. Name it in settings**

Go to **Settings -> Configuration**. At the foot of the **Repositories** card, under **Configuration repositories**, select **Add repository** and pick a repository. Repeat for up to three repositories.

**4. Confirm what Gitar found**

Saving reads the selected repositories and lists discovered skills under each repository. Connection and read failures appear separately for each selection.

The list shows skills only. Inherited rules and review instructions are not listed on the card, so confirm those on the next run in a repository that inherits them.

Select **Remove** beside a repository to stop inheriting from it. Select **Clear all** to stop inheriting shared configuration.

## Where Gitar looks

Automation rules come from `.gitar/rules/` and custom review instructions from `.gitar/review/`. Both are read recursively, so subdirectories work.

Gitar also reads the same [AI instruction files](/configuration/repository-config#ai-instruction-files) as in a local repository, including `CLAUDE.md`, `AGENTS.md`, `.cursorrules`, `.cursor/rules/` and `.claude/rules/`. These load automatically from the configuration repository's root. Both rule directories are read one level deep.

Keep shared Claude rules directly in `.claude/rules/`. No symlink to `.gitar/review/` is needed. These files provide instructions, while `.gitar/rules/` defines automation workflows.

Skills have six directories, in this order:

```text theme={null}
your-config-repo/
  .gitar/skills/<name>/SKILL.md
  .claude/skills/<name>/SKILL.md
  .agents/skills/<name>/SKILL.md
  .github/skills/<name>/SKILL.md
  skills/<name>/SKILL.md                     # marketplace layout
  plugins/<plugin>/skills/<name>/SKILL.md    # marketplace layout
```

The first four are the same directories Gitar reads in any repository. See [Where skills live](/integrations/skills#where-skills-live) for the precedence rule.

The last two are the Claude Code and Copilot marketplace layouts, read only in the configuration repository, so a marketplace repository works as-is.

A repository's own copy wins. Commit a skill under `.gitar/skills/` with the same `name` as an inherited one and the local version is what runs.

## When changes take effect

A push to the default branch can take up to 15 minutes to reach the next run.

To test instruction, rule, or skill edits before they land, open a PR in the configuration repository itself. Gitar reviews that PR using the version on its branch.

Approval and merge criteria still come from the default branch, so that PR cannot change its own approval or merge policy.

> [!WARNING]
>
> Anyone who can push to your configuration repository can change what every Gitar agent in your organization does. Skills, rules and review instructions are all instructions the agent follows, and a skill directory can carry scripts the agent runs.
>
> Protect its default branch the way you protect CI configuration: require review, and restrict who can merge.

## Troubleshooting

| The card says | Cause | Fix |
| - | - | - |
| "disconnected" beside a repository | The repository was disconnected from Gitar after it was named | Reconnect it, or name another repository |
| A read failure reason | Gitar could not fetch the repository | Remove and add the repository again to force a fresh read. Contact support if it persists |
| "No skills found" | Nothing under any of the six skill directories. Rules and review instructions are not counted here, so they may still be loading | Add `.gitar/skills/<name>/SKILL.md` and push, or ignore it if you only share rules and review instructions |

A failed fetch does not fail the review or prevent other selected repositories from loading. The card shows the connection state and the last save-time read.

### An inherited rule never runs

Check the repository under review for a rule file of the same name under `.gitar/rules/`, which takes precedence.

Then check that the repository runs its own rules at all. Inherited rules need the same plan as committed ones, so if `.gitar/rules/` in the repository does nothing, the configuration repository's will not run either.

See [Debugging](/features/rules#debugging) for the rest.

### An inherited skill never runs

Check the repository under review for a skill of the same `name`, which takes precedence. Then check the name against the slugs Gitar holds for [integrations](/integrations/custom-integrations), like `jira`, `linear` and `slack`. A skill named after one is dropped, whether or not you have that integration on, so rename it.

After that, see [Why is my skill not being used?](/integrations/skills#why-is-my-skill-not-being-used).


# Repository Customization
Source: https://docs.gitar.ai/configuration/repository-config

Configure Gitar's behavior at the repository level with instruction files, review rules, and the .gitar directory

Gitar reads its per-repository configuration out of your codebase: AI instruction files, custom review instructions, and automation rules. All of it is version-controlled alongside the code it applies to.

## AI Instruction Files

Gitar recognizes the following files as AI instruction sources:

| File | Description |
| - | - |
| `AGENTS.md` | General AI agent instructions for your repository. `agent.md` and `agents.md` are read too |
| `CLAUDE.md` | Claude-specific instructions and conventions |
| `.cursorrules` | Cursor-compatible instruction file |
| `.cursor/rules/*` | Directory of Cursor rule files |
| `.claude/rules/*` | Directory of rule files kept alongside `.claude/skills` |

Both rule directories are read one level deep, so a rule at `.claude/rules/backend/api.md` is not picked up. Keep rule files directly in the directory.

When Gitar processes a PR, it reads these files to understand your repository's conventions, coding standards, and best practices. This context is used during both code review and CI failure fixing.

> [!NOTE]
>
> These files are read automatically, with no additional configuration needed. Gitar loads them from your repository root on every PR. When the agent works inside a subdirectory during a run, it also picks up instruction files and rule directories between that subdirectory and the root.

### Including other files

If your conventions already live in another file, such as `guidelines.md`, reference it from any of the files above with `@` followed by its path. Gitar replaces the reference with that file's content.

```markdown AGENTS.md theme={null}
Follow the commit and naming conventions below.

@guidelines.md
@docs/architecture.md
```

Paths resolve from the folder of the file that contains them, then from the repository root, and must stay inside the repository. Put each reference on its own line or follow it with a space, since trailing punctuation becomes part of the path. References inside code blocks or inline code are ignored. Included files can reference other files as well.

You can also place the file in `.gitar/instructions/`, where every Markdown file loads for all Gitar agents.

## The .gitar Directory

The `.gitar` directory is the primary location for Gitar-specific configuration:

```text theme={null}
your-repo/
  .gitar/
    rules/        # Repository rules (automation workflows)
      *.md
    review/       # Custom code review instructions
      *.md
    skills/       # Reusable procedures the agent can run on request
      <name>/SKILL.md
    instructions/ # Instructions every Gitar agent reads, not just review
      *.md
    cross-repo.md # Guidance for Cross-Repo Analysis
    config/       # Per-repository criteria files
      apply.md
      approve.md
      merge.md
      risk.md
```

| Directory | Purpose |
| - | - |
| `.gitar/rules/` | Natural-language automation rules that trigger actions when conditions are met |
| `.gitar/review/` | Custom instructions that tailor what Gitar looks for during code review |
| `.gitar/skills/` | Named procedures loaded on demand. See [Skills](/integrations/skills) |
| `.gitar/instructions/` | Instructions loaded into every Gitar agent, not only code review |
| `.gitar/cross-repo.md` | Guidance for cross-repository checks. See [Cross-Repo Analysis](/features/cross-repo-analysis) |
| `.gitar/config/apply.md` | This repository's auto-apply criteria. See [Auto-Apply](/features/code-review/auto-apply) |
| `.gitar/config/approve.md` | This repository's auto-approve criteria. See [Auto-Approve](/features/code-review/auto-approve) |
| `.gitar/config/merge.md` | This repository's auto-merge criteria. See [Auto-Merge](/features/code-review/auto-merge) |
| `.gitar/config/risk.md` | Repository risk guidance that can raise the organization's assessment. See [Risk Triage](/features/code-review/risk-triage) |

`.claude/skills/`, `.agents/skills/`, and `.github/skills/` are read as well, so skills already kept for Claude Code, Codex, or Copilot need no second copy.

AI instruction files, skills, `.gitar/rules/`, `.gitar/review/`, `.gitar/instructions/` and `.gitar/cross-repo.md` can live in [configuration repositories](/configuration/configuration-repository) instead of being committed to each repository.

Select up to three configuration repositories to share configuration across the organization. Shared `approve.md`, `merge.md`, and `risk.md` files extend the organization baseline. Each repository's own files apply on top. `apply.md` stays per repository.

Gitar reads `apply.md`, `approve.md`, and `merge.md` from the default branch. Edits to those files take effect after they merge. Risk guidance also considers additions in the PR, as described in [Risk Triage](/features/code-review/risk-triage#add-repository-guidance).

## Custom Review Instructions

The `.gitar/review/` directory lets you tailor Gitar's code review to your project's specific requirements. Add one or more markdown files containing instructions, checks, or gotchas that Gitar should consider during reviews.

Multiple files can organize instructions by topic, for example separate files for security rules, performance guidelines, and project-specific gotchas.

**Example:** A file named `.gitar/review/gotchas.md` that documents codebase-specific patterns:

```markdown theme={null}
# Logging Gotchas

- Use `info!` for logging that requires operational visibility
- Leave `debug!` for local debugging only
- Our telemetry only renders logs with severity `info` or higher
```

### Including Specific Files

You can include content from other files using the `@` syntax with a relative path. Paths are first resolved relative to the source file, with a fallback to the repository root if not found.

**Example:** Given the following directory structure:

```text theme={null}
your-repo/
  .gitar/
    rust_best_practices.md
    review/
      gotchas.md
  shared/
    common_rules.md
```

From `.gitar/review/gotchas.md`, you can include files using either approach:

```markdown theme={null}
# Gotchas for Rust Code

@../rust_best_practices.md

@shared/common_rules.md

# Other project-specific gotchas
...
```

* `@../rust_best_practices.md` - resolved relative to `gotchas.md`, one level up from `review/`
* `@shared/common_rules.md` - not found relative to `review/`, so falls back to repo root resolution

This works recursively, so an included file can itself include other files.

## Repository Rules

Repository rules let you create automated workflows using natural language, specified in `.gitar/rules/*.md` files. When conditions defined in the rule are met on a PR, Gitar automatically executes the specified actions.

See the [Repository Rules documentation](/features/rules) for full details, syntax, and examples.

## Organization-Level Settings

Organization-level settings set the defaults every connected repository starts from. See [Settings](/configuration/settings) for what they cover.


# Organization Settings
Source: https://docs.gitar.ai/configuration/settings

Customize Gitar's Behavior

Open **Settings -> Configuration** in the dashboard to customize how Gitar operates across your organization. Behavior controls sit in the **Behavior** card.

## Configuration Scopes

Settings are configured at two levels:

* **Organization level**: sets the defaults for every repository connected to Gitar.
* **Repository level**: overrides specific org defaults for a single repo.

The org view is where you set defaults for behavior controls such as skip draft PRs, merge blocking, auto-approve, and auto-merge. When a repository needs different behavior, open its overrides dialog from the repository list. The dialog highlights which settings differ from the org default and lets you reset any override in one click.

> [!NOTE]
>
> Repository overrides only change settings that are explicitly toggled. Everything else inherits from the org default.

## Behavior

Behavior settings

### Skip drafts

When enabled, Gitar ignores draft PRs until they are marked ready for review.

### Disable automatic processing

When enabled, Gitar does nothing on its own for a PR. Opening, updating or labelling it starts no review, no CI analysis and no summary.
