* **A repository or project access token** (scoped to a single repo or project) - go to the repository or project's **Settings → Access tokens → Create token**. Use this if you want to limit Gitar's access to specific repositories rather than everything the service account can see.

Either way, grant **Read** and **Write** permission at the repository level, and **Project admin** if you want Gitar to register webhooks for you.

**Token expiry**

Set an expiration date and note it. Gitar stops working when the token expires and surfaces this in the dashboard as an unhealthy integration. To rotate, generate a new token with the same scopes, then **Disconnect** and reconnect the Server connection under **Settings → Configuration → Bitbucket → Server** with the new token.

**4. Connect in Gitar**

1. In the Gitar dashboard, go to **Settings → Configuration**
2. Click **Connect** next to Bitbucket, then open the **Server** tab
3. Enter your instance's base URL, for example `https://bitbucket.yourcompany.com`
4. Select the token type (personal, or repository/project) and paste the token
5. Select the projects you want Gitar to access
6. Click **Connect**

Gitar validates the token: reachability, permissions, and, if you plan to use auto-merge, that the account has a valid email. It then registers webhooks on the selected repositories automatically.

If the service account lacks Project Administrator permission, Gitar reports which repositories failed webhook setup. Grant the permission and reconnect to retry.

## Adding Repositories Later

Repositories created after you connect are not picked up automatically. Click **Refresh** on **Settings → Configuration → Repositories** to re-scan every connected instance.

On a service-account connection this also registers a webhook on each new repository, which is what makes Gitar see its pull requests at all - so run it after creating a repository, not only when the list looks stale. Repositories deleted since the last scan are dropped from the list.

## Network Reachability

Gitar's data plane must be able to reach your instance's REST API over HTTPS, and your instance must be able to send webhook requests to Gitar. If your instance enforces an IP allowlist, add Gitar's public IP addresses to it. See [Network Access](/connecting-code/network-access) for the addresses.

Gitar has no outbound-only tunnel, so an instance with no route to the public internet cannot connect. Contact us if that is your setup.

## Troubleshooting

> [accordion] The app upload button is missing or the upload is rejected
>
> On Bitbucket 10, app uploads are disabled by default and app signatures are checked. Add `-Dupm.plugin.upload.enabled=true` and `-Datlassian.upm.signature.check.disabled=true` to the JVM arguments and restart. If the upload is rejected as an incompatible plugin, you picked the wrong build - 10.x needs the Jakarta one.

> [accordion] Validation fails with a permissions error
>
> The service account (or the token's scope, if using a repository or project token) doesn't have sufficient permission on the project or repository. Grant at least Read and Write, and Project Administrator if you want Gitar to register webhooks for you.

> [accordion] Gitar doesn't see a repository I created
>
> Click **Refresh** on **Settings → Configuration → Repositories**. On a service-account connection, a new repository has no webhook until this runs, so it delivers no events at all.

> [accordion] Auto-merge doesn't work
>
> The service account has no email address set, or an invalid one. Bitbucket Data Center rejects merges from accounts without a valid email - set one on the service account and reconnect.

> [accordion] Gitar stops working after some time
>
> The access token has likely expired. Generate a new token with the same scopes, then **Disconnect** and reconnect the Server connection under **Settings → Configuration → Bitbucket → Server** with the new token.

> [accordion] Commits from Gitar show as unverified
>
> Gitar's signing key isn't registered on the bot account. The app registers it at connect time, so reconnecting through the app fixes this. On a service-account connection there is nothing to register the key, and commits stay unverified - this is cosmetic and doesn't affect anything else.

> [accordion] Webhooks aren't arriving
>
> Confirm your instance can reach Gitar's webhook endpoint over the public internet (or your configured private connection), and that no corporate proxy is stripping the `X-Hub-Signature` header. If the service account lacks Project Administrator permission, Gitar reports which repositories failed webhook setup. Grant the permission and reconnect to retry.

> [accordion] I changed my instance's base URL
>
> Changing the base URL, for example moving to a new domain, breaks the integration because Gitar uses it to route API calls and validate webhook signatures. Disconnect and reconnect with the new URL. If you connected through the app, the app reports the new URL itself and Gitar moves the existing connection over.

## Draft pull requests

The **Skip draft PRs** setting works on Bitbucket Data Center 10.x and later, which reports draft state on its pull request events. Earlier versions do not, so Gitar reviews drafts there regardless of the setting.


# GitHub
Source: https://docs.gitar.ai/connecting-code/github

Connect your GitHub repositories to Gitar

> [!NOTE]
>
> For what differs between this platform and the others, see [Platform Support](/connecting-code/platform-support).

GitHub repositories are connected to Gitar by installing the Gitar GitHub App. The app handles authentication, permissions, and webhook delivery, so there are no tokens or secrets to manage.

Connect GitHub

## Prerequisites

* A Gitar account with the organization admin role, signed in to the organization being connected
* Permission to install a GitHub App on the GitHub organization you want to connect

## Setup Instructions

> [!WARNING]
>
> Log into Gitar before you start the installation, and start it from **Settings -> Configuration** in Gitar or from the **Connect your repositories** onboarding step. Installing the app from GitHub Marketplace or a direct app link while signed out leaves the app installed on GitHub with nothing connected in Gitar.

**1. Access Gitar Dashboard**

In Gitar, go to **Settings -> Configuration**, or use the **Connect your repositories** onboarding step if you are still onboarding. Click **Connect** for GitHub to begin the installation process.

**2. Install the GitHub App**

You will be redirected to GitHub to install the Gitar App. Select the GitHub organization where you want to install the app.

**3. Select Repositories**

Choose which repositories Gitar should have access to. You can grant access to **all repositories** in the organization or **select specific ones**.

**Changing Repository Access Later**

You can modify which repositories the Gitar app can access at any time. Go to your GitHub organization **Settings -> Installed GitHub Apps -> Gitar -> Configure** to update repository access.

**4. Confirm Installation**

Review and approve the installation on GitHub. Once confirmed, you will be redirected back to Gitar with the connection active.

## Permissions

The installation asks you to grant two sets of permissions. Both are required.

**Read and Write Access to Code, Issues, and Pull Requests**

Gitar needs read and write access to your repository's code, issues, and pull requests. This enables Gitar to:

* Analyze your codebase and determine the changes it needs to make
* Create and manage pull requests and issues related to code maintenance tasks
* Push code changes to branches

Gitar **does not store your code**. Code is checked out, processed, and discarded.

**Read and Write Access to Actions, Checks, Commit Statuses, and Workflows**

Gitar requires both read and write access to actions, checks, commit statuses, and workflows to monitor and manage your repository's build process. This allows Gitar to:

* Introspect GitHub Actions logs and diagnose build failures
* Read PR comments and review feedback
* Fix build errors and address code review feedback automatically
* Improve the quality of its PRs by responding to CI results

## Supported GitHub Deployments

Gitar supports repositories hosted on [github.com](https://github.com). GitHub Enterprise Server is not supported.

For a self-hosted code host, Gitar supports [GitLab Self-Managed](/connecting-code/gitlab), [Azure DevOps Server](/connecting-code/azure-devops-server), and [Bitbucket Data Center](/connecting-code/bitbucket-data-center).

## Troubleshooting

> [accordion] I installed the app on GitHub but Gitar shows nothing connected
>
> This happens when the installation is started from GitHub while signed out of Gitar. GitHub shows the Gitar app installed, but Gitar has no record of it, so pull requests in those repositories are ignored.
>
> To recover, uninstall the app from your GitHub organization under **Settings -> Installed GitHub Apps -> Gitar -> Uninstall**. Then log into Gitar and start the installation again from **Settings -> Configuration**, or from the **Connect your repositories** onboarding step if you have not finished onboarding.

> [accordion] The app connected to the wrong Gitar organization
>
> An installation binds to the Gitar organization active in your session when GitHub redirects you back, not to the GitHub organization you installed it on. If you belong to more than one Gitar organization, switch to the right one before starting the install.
>
> To move an existing installation, uninstall it on GitHub, switch to the correct organization in Gitar, then install again from **Settings -> Configuration**.

> [accordion] Gitar isn't processing my PRs
>
> Verify that the GitHub App is installed on the correct organization and has access to the repository in question. In your GitHub organization, go to **Settings -> Installed GitHub Apps** and confirm the Gitar app is listed and configured for the expected repositories.

> [accordion] Permission errors when Gitar pushes
>
> Confirm the GitHub App has write access to the repository. If you are using branch protection rules, you may need to add the Gitar app to the bypass list so it can push commits and update protected branches.

> [accordion] I need to change which repositories Gitar accesses
>
> Go to your GitHub organization **Settings -> Installed GitHub Apps -> Gitar -> Configure** to modify repository access. Changes take effect immediately.


# GitLab
Source: https://docs.gitar.ai/connecting-code/gitlab

Connect your GitLab repositories to Gitar

> [!NOTE]
>
> For what differs between this platform and the others, see [Platform Support](/connecting-code/platform-support).

Gitar connects to GitLab using a **personal access token (PAT)**. A **GitLab service account** is the preferred way to generate this token; a dedicated user's PAT works as a fallback, but that approach occupies a seat on your GitLab instance and its token stops working if that user ever leaves. Gitar posts comments and pushes changes under that account's identity, so use an account reserved for Gitar.

## Prerequisites

* GitLab.com, or GitLab Self-Managed or Dedicated version 16.0 or later. Community Edition and GitLab Free have no service account feature and must use a dedicated user's PAT instead.
* A service account (preferred) or dedicated user, with access to the group and the Maintainer or Owner role on the projects being connected.

**Gitar registers one webhook per project**

Gitar registers a webhook on each project as you connect it, on every tier, and never on the group. A project webhook carries every event Gitar consumes and needs only the Maintainer role. One consequence: connect projects you add later from the project browser, since a per-project webhook cannot cover a project that does not exist yet.

## Setup Instructions

**1. Choose an account**

> [accordion] GitLab service account (recommended)
>
> 1. Go to your group in GitLab
> 2. On the left sidebar, select **Settings → Service accounts**
> 3. Select **Add service account**
> 4. Enter a name and username, then select **Create**
>
> GitLab Create service account dialog with a name and username filled in
>
> On Self-Managed and Dedicated, an administrator may need to create the account or allow group Owners to do so. Use a unique username such as `gitar-service-acme`.
>
> A service account is the preferred option: unlike a regular user, it does not occupy a seat on your GitLab instance, and its token keeps working even if the person who set it up leaves.
>
> **Where you create the account decides where it can be used**
>
> A service account can only be invited to the group or project that created it, and to anything beneath it. Creating the account in your **top-level group** keeps every subgroup and project available to you, including any you decide to connect later.
>
> Group and project service accounts stay within the namespace that created them. Choose the top-level group when Gitar needs access across its subgroups.
>
> On GitLab Self-Managed and Dedicated, an administrator can instead create an instance service account from the Admin area, which can be invited to any group on the instance.

> [accordion] Dedicated user (fallback), including Community Edition
>
> Use this path only when the instance has no **Service accounts** menu, since a dedicated user works without GitLab's service account feature. Create a regular GitLab user reserved for Gitar, such as `gitar-bot-acme`.
>
> This approach is a fallback, not the preferred option: a dedicated user occupies a normal seat on the GitLab instance, and its PAT becomes invalid if that user's account is ever removed or deactivated. Prefer a service account whenever your GitLab edition and version support one.
>
> Keep this account separate from daily development. Gitar treats comments from the connected account as its own and does not respond to them.

**2. Generate a personal access token**

For a service account, open **Settings → Service accounts**, then select **Manage access tokens** next to the account. For a dedicated user, sign in as that user and open its [personal access tokens](https://docs.gitlab.com/user/profile/personal_access_tokens/#create-a-personal-access-token) page instead.

1. Open the account's access token page
2. Select **Add new token**
3. Enter a token name and set an expiration date. Note this date so you can rotate the token before it expires
4. Select the `api` scope, then select **Generate token**
5. Copy the token immediately, as GitLab will not show it again

> [!WARNING]
>
> Group and project access tokens are not supported. Generate the PAT from the service account (preferred) or dedicated user that Gitar will act as.

The `api` scope covers repository access and API reads and writes, including posting comments and setting up webhooks. Read-only scopes combined with `write_repository` do not permit these API writes.

**3. Invite the account to your group**

Invite the service account or dedicated user to the group Gitar will work in:

1. Go to that group in GitLab
2. On the left sidebar, select **Manage → Members**
3. Select **Invite members**
4. Search for the account, assign the **Maintainer** or **Owner** role, then select **Invite**

GitLab Invite members dialog with the Gitar service account selected and the Owner role assigned

Maintainer is enough for project webhooks and review actions, including pushing fix branches. Merge blocking depends on GitLab's approval-rule support, not on whether you chose a service account or a dedicated user.

**Connecting a subgroup**

Invite the account to the group whose path you enter in the next step. Membership inherits downwards, so inviting it to a parent group also covers every subgroup and project beneath it. Inviting it to a subgroup gives it no access to the parent.

**4. Connect in Gitar**

Create one connection per GitLab host in each Gitar organization. The same connection can cover multiple top-level groups that the connected account can access. After connecting, select those groups under [Configure Repository Access](#configure-repository-access).

Use the generated token to configure the connection with the following fields:

* **GitLab Host**: The hostname of your GitLab instance, for example gitlab.com or gitlab.example.com
* **Group/Namespace Path**: The full path of the group, including any parent groups. `acme` for gitlab.com/acme, or `acme/platform` for a subgroup
* **Project ID (Optional)**: Find your project ID in GitLab project settings
* **Personal Access Token**: The token you generated above, with the `api` scope

Gitar validates the token as you enter it and reports any problem with the host, the token, or access to the group.

Gitar token validation showing a service account PAT with the api scope and all checks passing

**Changing Configuration After Setup**

Open **Settings → Configuration → Connections**, then select **Configure** for the GitLab connection to update its token or add groups and projects.

## Configure Repository Access

To onboard everything under the connected GitLab group, select that parent group in **Groups**. Gitar connects all existing projects in the group and every subgroup the connected account can access. There is no need to add each subgroup or project separately.

1. Open **Settings → Configuration → Connections** and select **Configure** for the existing GitLab connection.
2. Keep the default **Groups** tab selected.
3. Search for the top-level group's full path, such as `acme`, and select the matching group.
4. Review the project preview, then select **Onboard N projects**. Gitar includes projects in subgroups such as `acme/platform` and `acme/mobile`, subject to the connected account's permissions.

For individual repositories, switch to **Projects**, search by name or full project path, and select **Add**, then **Save**. The screenshot below shows the Projects view with the earlier tab order. **Groups** opens by default on the left, with **Projects** on the right.

Projects view in Configure GitLab showing individual repositories with Add and Disconnect buttons

### Multiple Top-Level Groups on One Host

Reuse the existing connection for each top-level group on the same GitLab host. Reopen **Configure → Groups** and repeat the steps above for each group. A second connection to the same host is rejected.

The connected account must have access to every group being added. A group-created service account cannot span unrelated top-level groups. On Self-Managed or Dedicated, use an instance service account invited to each group. A dedicated user's PAT is the fallback when service accounts are unavailable.

> [!NOTE]
>
> One connection does not automatically connect an entire GitLab instance. Select each top-level group explicitly. Projects created later must also be connected from **Configure**.

## Merge Blocking

When Gitar's review finds unresolved blocking findings, it creates a merge-request approval rule requiring its own approval, which GitLab enforces on the merge button.

Approval rules are enforced on Premium and Ultimate only. An instance without them accepts the rule and then ignores it, so Gitar checks which kind of instance yours is when you connect and on every refresh.

Where rules are not enforced, Gitar skips the rule and does not claim a block that is not there. The connection in **Settings → Configuration → Connections** says merge blocking is unavailable. Reviews and findings are unaffected.

To gate merges without approval rules, add a job that calls the merge-request status endpoint and fails when Gitar reports the merge request as blocked. See [Get MR Status](/api-reference/gitlab/get-mr-status) for the endpoint and an example job.

## Self-Hosted GitLab: Git Hooks Configuration

If your self-hosted GitLab instance uses server-side git hooks to validate committer emails (push rules or custom hooks), you must allow Gitar's committer email address.

> [!WARNING]
>
> Gitar uses **[noreply@gitar.ai](mailto:noreply@gitar.ai)** as the committer email when pushing changes, including fix requests and automated commits. If this email is not allowed, Gitar cannot push and those operations fail.

### Adding the Gitar Committer Email

Depending on your GitLab configuration, add `noreply@gitar.ai` to your allowed committer list:

**Option 1: GitLab Push Rules (Recommended)**

1. Go to **Admin Area → Push Rules** (for instance-wide rules) or **Project Settings → Repository → Push Rules** (for project-specific rules)
2. In the "Commit author's email" field, add a regex pattern that includes Gitar's email:
   ```text theme={null}
   @yourcompany\.com$|^noreply@gitar\.ai$
   ```
3. Save the push rules

**Option 2: Custom Server-Side Hooks**

If you use custom `pre-receive` or `update` hooks that validate committer emails, add `noreply@gitar.ai` to your allowed email list in the hook script.

**Option 3: Connected Account Email Configuration**

Add `noreply@gitar.ai` to the connected GitLab account as one of its verified emails.

## Firewall / IP Allowlisting

If your GitLab group or instance enforces an IP allowlist, add every address listed
in [Network Access](/connecting-code/network-access).

After changing the allowlist, open **Settings → Configuration** in Gitar and click
**Refresh** in the Repositories card to retry webhook registration.

## Troubleshooting

**"GitLab at … is already connected to this organization"**

Open the existing connection's **Configure → Groups** tab to add another group on that host. A separate connection is not needed. If the group cannot be found, check the connected account's membership and whether its service account is restricted to another top-level group.

**"The group or namespace was not found"**

Gitar could not read the group with the token you supplied. GitLab returns the same response whether the group does not exist or the token cannot see it, so check both:

* The path is the group's **full path**, including any parent groups. A subgroup is `parent-group/subgroup`, not `subgroup`.
* The connected account is a member of that group or one of its parent groups. Creating a service account inside a group does not grant membership on its own.

**"Group or project access tokens are not supported"**

Generate a PAT from the service account’s **Manage access tokens** page (preferred), or the dedicated user’s profile as a fallback. Tokens generated from group or project **Access tokens** settings are not accepted.

**No Service accounts menu**

Service accounts are preferred but not required. Use a dedicated regular user and generate its PAT instead — availability of the Service accounts menu depends on the GitLab edition and version.


# Network Access
Source: https://docs.gitar.ai/connecting-code/network-access

Firewall and IP allowlisting requirements for connecting Gitar to your code host

If your code host enforces an IP allowlist, permit traffic from Gitar's public
addresses. That covers a self-hosted instance behind a firewall, and a cloud instance
with allowlisting turned on, such as GitHub or GitLab organization-level IP
restrictions.

Every outbound request Gitar makes leaves from these, including connection
validation, repository clones, pushes, webhook registration, and code host API calls.

## IPv4

Outbound IPv4 is translated to one of three addresses, one per availability zone:

```text theme={null}
44.208.227.28
44.221.203.128
44.213.34.15
```

Add all three. Gitar spreads work across availability zones and fails over between
them, so an allowlist holding a subset breaks intermittently rather than cleanly.

## IPv6

Gitar reaches code hosts over IPv4 only, from the addresses above. If your allowlist
still holds Gitar's former IPv6 range, `2600:1f28:74:4000::/60`, remove it. Gitar keeps
the range reserved for now, so it is not reissued while allowlists are updated, but it
will be released later.

> [!NOTE]
>
> The IPv4 addresses are stable. They are Elastic IPs, held independently of the
> instances that use them, so they survive instance replacement and routine
> maintenance.
>
> They apply to every supported code host: GitHub, GitLab, Azure DevOps, and
> Bitbucket, and are production-only. Gitar's staging environment egresses from a
> different set, which is not customer-facing.


# Platform Support
Source: https://docs.gitar.ai/connecting-code/platform-support

Which Gitar features work on each code host, and what differs

Gitar reviews pull requests on GitHub, GitLab, Azure DevOps, and Bitbucket. AWS CodeCommit is available in preview for Enterprise organizations. Contact your enterprise account manager to request access. Code review, rules, and CI failure analysis run on every host.

What differs is mostly merge control, plus how a fix is applied and whether the host shows a verified badge. The table below is the full picture.

## Feature support

> [!NOTE]
>
> ● supported  ·  ◐ supported with a difference, see the note  ·  ○ not available

| | GitHub | GitLab | Azure DevOps | Bitbucket Cloud | Bitbucket Data Center | AWS CodeCommit Preview |
| - | :-: | :-: | :-: | :-: | :-: | :-: |
| **Review** | | | | | | |
| Code review with inline findings | ● | ● | ● | ● | ● | ◐ <sup>11</sup> |
| [Agentic Security](/features/agentic-security) | ● | ● | ● | ● | ● | ◐ <sup>11</sup> |
| Suggested fixes | ● | ● | ● | ● | ● | ● |
| One-click apply | ● <sup>1</sup> | ● <sup>1</sup> | ○ <sup>2</sup> | ○ <sup>2</sup> | ○ <sup>2</sup> | ○ <sup>2</sup> |
| Rules | ● | ● | ● | ● | ● | ● |
| PR summaries | ● | ● | ● | ● | ● | ● |
| Cross-repo analysis | ● | ● | ● | ● | ● | ● |
| **Merge control** | | | | | | |
| Block merge | ● | ◐ <sup>3</sup> | ● | ◐ <sup>4</sup> | ◐ <sup>4</sup> | ● <sup>13</sup> |
| Auto-approve | ● | ● | ● | ● | ● | ○ |
| Auto-merge | ● | ● | ● | ○ <sup>5</sup> | ◐ <sup>6</sup> | ○ |
| Choose the merge method | ● | ○ <sup>7</sup> | ● | ○ | ○ | ○ |
| **CI** | | | | | | |
| CI failure analysis | ● | ● | ● | ● | ◐ <sup>8</sup> | ● <sup>12</sup> |
| Connected CI providers | ● | ● | ● | ● | ● | ◐ <sup>12</sup> |
| **Commits** | | | | | | |
| Signed fix commits | ● | ● | ● | ● | ● | ● |
| Verified badge on the commit | ● | ● | ○ <sup>9</sup> | ○ <sup>9</sup> | ● <sup>10</sup> | ○ <sup>9</sup> |

<sup>1</sup> See [Code Review](/features/code-review#providing-feedback) for how each host applies a fix.

<sup>2</sup> Reply `gitar fix` on the finding. On Azure DevOps Server, follow the [comment checkbox instructions](/connecting-code/azure-devops-server#comments-and-commands) when the checkbox is present.

<sup>3</sup> Only where the instance enforces merge-request approval rules; elsewhere Gitar places no gate and posts no blocking comment. See [Block Merge](/features/code-review/block-merge).

<sup>4</sup> Bitbucket records the review and shows it, and does not treat it as a merge gate. A person can still merge.

<sup>5</sup> Bitbucket Cloud has no native auto-merge to arm.

<sup>6</sup> Data Center has no "merge when ready" flag. Gitar watches the PR and merges it once Bitbucket's own merge checks pass, so there is no armed state in the Bitbucket UI.

<sup>7</sup> GitLab merges using the project's own merge-method setting.

<sup>8</sup> Data Center reports build status on a poll rather than a push, so analysis starts a little later than on the other hosts.

<sup>9</sup> The commit is signed and verifies locally, with no badge on the platform. See [Signed Commits](/configuration/signed-commits).

<sup>10</sup> Gitar registers its key when you connect. See [Signed Commits](/configuration/signed-commits).

<sup>11</sup> CodeCommit drops PR comment threads from the **Changes** tab after a fresh commit. But the **Activity** tab still preserves context and interaction with Gitar.

<sup>12</sup> CodeCommit CI analysis supports CodeBuild, including CodeBuild actions in CodePipeline. CI analysis is selected by default during onboarding, which supplies the required AWS permissions and configures event delivery. Other CodePipeline action providers are not supported. See [CodeCommit setup](/connecting-code/aws-codecommit).

<sup>13</sup> CodeCommit uses a native approval rule to hold the merge. Gitar removes its own rule when the review clears or an authorized user runs `gitar unblock`. See [Block Merge](/features/code-review/block-merge).

[Concourse](/connecting-ci/concourse) and [Woodpecker](/connecting-ci/woodpecker) CI connections support GitHub repositories. See each setup guide for server requirements.

## Self-hosted and server editions

| Deployment | Supported |
| - | - |
| GitLab self-managed, including Free | ● [Guide](/connecting-code/gitlab) |
| Azure DevOps Server | ● [Guide](/connecting-code/azure-devops-server) |
| Bitbucket Data Center | ● [Guide](/connecting-code/bitbucket-data-center) |
| GitHub Enterprise Server | ○ |

A self-hosted server has to be reachable from Gitar and able to send webhooks out. See [Network Access](/connecting-code/network-access).

# Agentic Security
Source: https://docs.gitar.ai/features/agentic-security

Review changes to agent instructions and supporting scripts for harmful behavior

Agentic Security brings [Sonar's OpenAI Daybreak collaboration](https://www.sonarsource.com/blog/sonar-joins-openai-daybreak-program/) to Gitar's [code reviews](/features/code-review). It catches changes to agent skills that could leak credentials or private code, or remove a required approval step.

Gitar reviews instructions together with the scripts they call. Comparing instruction versions can reveal a dropped sign-off step even when the publishing command stays the same. Gitar inspects suspicious content without running it and validates each issue before posting a finding.

Agentic Security is included with [Enterprise](/account-billing/plans#feature-comparison).

## What it reviews

Gitar examines changed agent behavior in files such as `SKILL.md`, `AGENTS.md`, and `CLAUDE.md`, along with equivalent agent configuration. It follows relevant local references and helper scripts to understand what the instructions would cause an agent to do.

A helper script can change agent behavior even when the instruction file stays the same. Gitar checks the connection between the changed helper and the instructions that invoke it.

| Changed behavior | What Gitar looks for |
| - | - |
| Uploading credentials or private source. | Disclosure to an unauthorized destination. |
| Running commands through an instruction or helper. | Concealed execution outside the legitimate task. |
| Changing approval or access checks. | Actions that bypass required authorization. |
| Directing how an agent reports its review. | Instructions that hide issues or manipulate review results. |

Ordinary authentication and authorized deployment are legitimate uses of agent instructions. Quoted attack examples or destructive commands need evidence of harmful behavior outside their authorized purpose before Gitar reports an issue.

## How review works

During a code review, Gitar inspects the changed instructions and follows the affected local paths. The review focuses on behavior introduced by the PR, including changes made through referenced scripts.

Supported issues appear as security findings in the normal code review. Each finding identifies the changed location and explains the path from the instruction to the harmful action.

Use the existing [feedback and resolution flow](/features/code-review#providing-feedback) to respond to findings. Subsequent reviews can resolve issues after the relevant behavior is corrected.

## Inspect security findings

Open **Dashboard -> Agentic Security** to inspect critical and important security findings across the organization. The page brings together high-impact security findings from Gitar's code reviews, including the instruction reviewer.

Agentic Security dashboard with totals for open critical and important findings, fixed findings, and affected repositories above the findings list

The initial view shows open findings across all PR states. Critical findings appear first. Use the repository and date filters to focus the list, or select **Fixed** to inspect issues that have been resolved.

Expand **Evidence and suggested fix** to read the explanation. The link icon after each title opens the posted finding comment, or the original change when no comment link is available.

The severity filter offers **Critical**, **Important**, or both. Lower-severity findings remain available in [Review findings](/features/code-review#review-output).

## Review coverage

The inspection follows affected instructions and their local references. Unrelated unchanged instructions do not expand the review into an audit of the entire repository.

Gitar inspects instructions and supporting code without running the commands or payloads under review. An unavailable reference can limit the conclusions supported by the review.

## Related

> [card] [Code Review](/features/code-review)
>
> How findings are reported and resolved.

> [card] [Skills](/integrations/skills)
>
> Define procedures that agents can follow in a repository.


# CI Failure Analysis
Source: https://docs.gitar.ai/features/ci-failure-analysis

Automatic analysis and fixes for CI failures on your pull requests

## Overview

Gitar monitors your CI pipelines and automatically analyzes failures on every PR. It identifies root causes, posts detailed breakdowns, and can push fixes.

## Which CI Platforms Need Connecting

Gitar reads CI logs so review and repair can follow the pipelines attached to the repository.

Gitar reads GitHub Actions, GitLab Pipelines, Bitbucket Pipelines, and Azure DevOps
Pipelines with no setup, using the credential from your code host connection.

The [AWS CodeCommit preview](/connecting-code/aws-codecommit) also supports CodeBuild and CodePipeline analysis when CI access is granted during setup.

Every other CI platform reports its builds to the pull request as a status check, and
Gitar needs its own credential to follow that link and fetch the log. Connect the ones in use:

| Platform | Guide |
| - | - |
| Jenkins | [Connect Jenkins](/connecting-ci/jenkins) |
| CircleCI | [Connect CircleCI](/connecting-ci/circleci) |
| Concourse (GitHub) | [Connect Concourse](/connecting-ci/concourse) |
| Woodpecker (GitHub) | [Connect Woodpecker](/connecting-ci/woodpecker) |
| Buildkite | [Connect Buildkite](/connecting-ci/buildkite) |
| TeamCity | [Connect TeamCity](/connecting-ci/teamcity) |
| Harness | [Connect Harness](/connecting-ci/harness) |
| Bitrise | [Connect Bitrise](/connecting-ci/bitrise) |

## How It Works

When CI fails on a PR, Gitar automatically:

**1. Analyze the failure**

Gitar reads the CI logs, identifies the failing step, and determines the root cause of the failure.

**2. Post an analysis comment**

A detailed explanation of the failure is posted to the dashboard comment on your PR, including which files and lines are involved.

**3. Suggest or apply a fix**

Depending on your auto-apply setting, Gitar either waits for your approval or pushes a fix commit directly to the branch.

The analysis appears in the [Gitar dashboard comment](/gitar-dashboard) on your PR.

The analysis covers your full CI pipeline output, so Gitar can diagnose failures across multiple jobs and steps in a single run. Identical failures across multiple jobs are deduplicated so they only get analyzed once. CI events for outdated commits are ignored after you push new code.

| Failure Type | Examples |
| - | - |
| **Build errors** | Compilation failures, missing imports, type errors |
| **Test failures** | Broken assertions, missing setup, incorrect expected values |
| **Linting errors** | Code style violations, formatting issues, static analysis warnings |
| **Flaky tests** | Race conditions, timing issues, non-deterministic behavior |

## Applying Fixes

After Gitar posts its analysis, you have three options:

| Option | How |
| - | - |
| **Let Gitar auto-fix** | Enable auto-apply on the PR with `gitar auto-apply:on`. Gitar pushes a fix commit for failures confirmed as caused by the PR. |
| **Fix it yourself** | Use the root cause analysis as context and push your own fix. Gitar updates its analysis when new commits arrive. |
| **Request a targeted fix** | Comment `Gitar <specific instruction>` on the PR to tell Gitar exactly how you want the issue resolved. |

With auto-apply on, Gitar automatically fixes only failures its analysis confirms were caused by the PR. It skips automatic fixes when the cause is unrelated or uncertain. You can still request a fix explicitly with `Gitar fix CI`.

Auto-apply can be toggled at any time with `gitar auto-apply:on` / `gitar auto-apply:off`. See [Commands & Interactions](/commands) for all available commands.

## CI Retry for Unrelated Failures

Gitar can automatically retry CI jobs associated with unrelated failures, such as flaky tests or transient infrastructure failures. Related or uncertain findings do not trigger retries. Turn on **Retry unrelated CI failures** under **Settings -> Configuration -> Behavior**, independent from auto-apply.

Retry works on GitHub Actions, GitLab Pipelines, Bitbucket Pipelines, Azure DevOps Pipelines, and CircleCI. CircleCI reruns the whole workflow. Gitar avoids repeatedly retrying the same failed run. On Bitbucket, a retry creates a new pipeline, and Gitar does not automatically retry that pipeline again.

In the AWS CodeCommit preview, Gitar retries standalone CodeBuild builds. For CodePipeline, it retries a stage only when every failed action is unrelated to the PR. Other connected CI platforms get failure analysis without retry.

On Azure DevOps Cloud and Server, the PAT needs **Build (Read & execute)** for retries. **Build (Read)** is enough for analysis. See the [Cloud](/connecting-code/azure-devops#build-read) or [Server](/connecting-code/azure-devops-server#build-read) setup guide.

## Recovery after a Gitar fix

When Gitar breaks previously passing CI, it can attempt a follow-up repair with auto-apply off. Recovery requires the latest failure to be related to the PR, with one or two consecutive Gitar commits after a commit whose CI passed.

This recovery is separate from retrying an unrelated flaky job. See [CI Retry for Unrelated Failures](#ci-retry-for-unrelated-failures) for that setting.

## Multi-Iteration Fixing

CI failures may not be resolved in a single pass. Gitar supports multi-iteration fixing:

1. Gitar pushes a fix for the original CI failure.
2. CI re-runs on the updated branch.
3. If CI fails again (whether from the same issue or one the fix introduced), Gitar re-analyzes the new failure.
4. Gitar attempts another fix, taking into account the full history of previous attempts.

Further automatic fixes depend on auto-apply or eligibility for recovery. Recovery stops after three consecutive Gitar commits.

If CI still fails, review Gitar’s analysis and add a PR comment starting with `Gitar` to request another fix:

```text theme={null}
Gitar fix CI
```

Gitar reads the CI logs from connected platforms and analyzes the failure. You can also include context or constraints, such as an environment requirement or a timeout that should stay unchanged. If you provide additional log output, include only the relevant excerpt and remove secrets, credentials, and other sensitive information before posting.

You can also push a correction yourself.


# Code Review
Source: https://docs.gitar.ai/features/code-review

Review code behavior with AST-informed context, team knowledge, and checks across repositories

## Overview

Gitar reviews PRs using the surrounding code and the team's engineering context. Findings land on the changed lines, with explanations and suggested fixes.

> [card] Security Analysis
>
> Vulnerabilities, unsafe patterns, input validation

> [card] Bug Detection
>
> Logic errors, null pointer risks, edge cases

> [card] Performance
>
> Algorithm complexity, database queries, memory usage

> [card] Code Quality
>
> Readability, maintainability, best practices

## Beyond the changed lines

Gitar uses [AST-informed code structure](/how-gitar-works#code-structure-informs-the-review) to navigate the implementation, then reads related files and searches for callers to investigate the effect of a change.

| Coverage | What it adds to the review |
| - | - |
| [Agentic Security](/features/agentic-security) | Security review of changes to agent instructions and supporting scripts. |
| [Knowledge](/features/knowledge) | Team conventions learned from instructions and human review feedback, with source citations. |
| [Cross-Repo Analysis](/features/cross-repo-analysis) | Impacts on other repositories, including dependencies across code hosts. |
| [Cross-PR Analysis](/features/cross-pr-analysis) | Logic mismatches between related open PRs, with references to both changes. |
| [Functional Validation](/features/functional-validation) | Objective-by-objective checks against linked issue requirements. |
| [Risk Triage](/features/code-review/risk-triage) | A whole-PR risk assessment shaped by organization and repository policy. |

See [How Gitar Works](/how-gitar-works) for the context used during review, including web documentation and connected knowledge sources.

## Large PRs and review volume

Gitar supports unlimited changed files per PR. Large changes remain eligible for review, including changes spread across a monorepo.

Gitar prioritizes relevant code and condenses repetitive changes to keep large diffs reviewable. Generated files and other low-value content may be excluded from review.

Enterprise includes [unlimited review volume per author](/account-billing/plans#enterprise-scale), including allowed bot authors. Other plans follow the [usage policy](/account-billing/plans#usage-limits).

## How It Works

Code reviews run automatically when:

* A new pull request is created
* New commits are pushed to an open PR

Gitar skips a PR containing only a single revert commit. This also applies when the original change was never reviewed by Gitar.

Push any commit beyond the revert and Gitar reviews the pull request as usual, because at that point it is no longer a plain revert. Everything else still runs either way, including CI analysis and rule evaluation.

## Review depth

Review depth controls which verified findings Gitar reports.

| Setting | What to expect |
| - | - |
| Focused | Prioritizes higher-impact findings. |
| Thorough | Includes smaller verified issues, with more comments to assess. |

Enterprise organization admins choose the default under **Settings -> Configuration -> Review depth**. To set a different depth for a repository, select **Customize** on its row and choose **Focused** or **Thorough**. Changes take effect on the next review, including existing PRs.

Repositories inherit the organization default until an override is saved. Resetting an override restores inheritance. Previously saved review depth settings are retained.

## Custom Code Review Instructions

Add your own rules, checks, or gotchas to tailor the review to your project. Commit a markdown file under the **`.gitar/review`** directory. Use several files to organize instructions by topic.

Gitar's own repository, for example, has a `gitar-gotchas.md` covering conventions specific to that codebase. One of them is to use `info!` for logging that needs operational visibility and leave `debug!` for local debugging, since its telemetry only renders logs at severity `info` or higher.

One file can pull in another with the `@` syntax, so shared guidance lives in one place. See [Custom Review Instructions](/configuration/repository-config#custom-review-instructions) for the directory layout and include resolution.

## Review Output

Gitar publishes review feedback in two places on your PR:

* **Inline review comments**: every unresolved finding is posted on the exact file and line it applies to, so feedback lands where you're reading the code.
* **Dashboard comment, Code Review section**: a consolidated view on the Gitar dashboard comment showing the overall verdict, severity breakdown, and resolved-finding tracking.

The dashboard comment is the single source of truth for the overall state of a PR, holding the code review summary, CI analysis, and rule evaluations in one place. When you push new commits, Gitar updates that comment instead of posting a new one, so your PR timeline stays clean.

Resolved findings move into a collapsible section as you push fixes, so what is left to address stays at the top.

The **Cross-Repo** and **Cross-PR** categories only appear when [Cross-Repo Analysis](/features/cross-repo-analysis) or [Cross-PR Analysis](/features/cross-pr-analysis) is turned on for your organization.

To change how that summary reads, set [review summary instructions](/configuration/settings#review-summary-instructions) for your organization.

Code Review in Dashboard Comment

Each finding includes:

* **Category**: Security, Bug, Performance, Edge Case, Code Quality, Cross-Repo, or Cross-PR
* **Severity**: Critical, Important, or Minor
* **Short description**: One-line summary of the issue
* **Details**: Expanded explanation with a link to the specific file and line
* **Citations**: links to available sources for learned guidance. See [Citations in findings](#citations-in-findings).

### Citations in findings

Citations make team-specific review advice traceable. When a finding relies on learned guidance, its explanation can link to the instruction or earlier conversation behind that guidance.

The link appears within the finding, alongside the reasoning about the changed code. See [Knowledge source citations](/features/knowledge#source-citations) for how sources are retained and when a citation becomes a link.

### Verification

**Verification** follows Code Review and collects recorded outcomes, including linked issue objectives and rule results. It can appear without a code review. Covered objectives and checked expectations describe assessments against code, without claiming that runtime tests ran.

The header keeps one compact badge per feature, such as `Objectives 5/7`, `Rules 3/4 met` and `Repos 1 inspected`. Knowledge shows check and lens counts. The rule ratio excludes skipped checks and recorded actions.

The section opens for unmet requirements. A knowledge-backed finding opens it while that finding remains open in the current review. Settled outcomes collapse, even when Code Review still requests changes.

| Row | What it reports |
| - | - |
| Functional validation | Active objective counts, linked issues and objective names. |
| Rules | Check outcomes and recorded actions, with successful names visible in normal mode. |
| Auto-approval | Approved, Withheld or Not evaluated. |
| Knowledge | Expectations explicitly met and retained finding citations, with finding links. |
| Cross-repo | Recorded inspections and open impacts, or selected repositories when inspection status is unknown. |

Each feature has its own collapsed section and outcome badges. Open a feature to read up to five successful names. Unmet requirements stay visible without another toggle. `gitar display:verbose` adds remaining names and supporting evidence. Platforms without collapsible HTML show the same results as headings and compact rows.

Code Review still requests changes while Verification shows four covered objectives after opening its feature section

The [risk label](/features/code-review/risk-triage#where-risk-appears) appears separately in expanded Code Review, since risk describes the whole change rather than which checks ran.

## Providing Feedback

Feedback controls how Gitar handles findings on the current PR:

* Reply to a finding with an explanation. A confirmed fix resolves it. An invalid finding is dismissed. A decision to leave it unfixed closes it without counting it as fixed. On GitLab, replies to inline findings need no `gitar` prefix.
* **Reply `gitar fix`**: Every inline finding includes a "Reply `gitar fix` to apply this suggestion" footer. One reply and Gitar pushes the fix.
* **One-click apply**: On GitHub, check the box next to a suggested fix to apply it. On GitLab, react with the checkmark emoji. Gitar commits the fix to your branch. When a finding has multiple fixes, each one is numbered.
* Resolving a finding thread on GitHub closes it without marking it fixed or invalid. Unresolving that thread reopens it. On GitLab, a thumbs-down reaction dismisses a finding.
* **Stale findings**: When code is deleted or rewritten between review iterations, Gitar automatically resolves findings that pointed at the removed code.
* **Ambiguous replies**: If your reply is unclear, Gitar asks a follow-up question instead of guessing.

Acknowledging a finding or promising a follow-up keeps it open. Acknowledged findings still count toward merge blocking and do not count as fixed. The [Reviews dashboard](/features/insights#reviews) shows their recorded reasons and source replies when available.

If an accepted fix fails, the inline comment shows **Fix could not be applied**. The failed attempt does not automatically start another one. Request another fix in a reply or push a correction.

Your feedback persists across review iterations. Gitar won't re-report issues you've already dismissed.

With [Knowledge](/features/knowledge) enabled, explanations in review comments can also teach Gitar conventions for future PRs.

## Merge Blocking

Gitar can block PR merges based on its code review verdict severity. Configure a threshold in your [organization settings](/configuration/settings), and Gitar submits a blocking review on the PR when the verdict meets or exceeds it. See [Block Merge](/features/code-review/block-merge) for details.

## Auto-Approve

Gitar can automatically approve PRs after a clean review based on criteria you define. See [Auto-Approve](/features/code-review/auto-approve) for details.

## Auto-Merge

After auto-approve, Gitar can arm your platform's native auto-merge so the PR merges once all other checks pass. See [Auto-Merge](/features/code-review/auto-merge) for details.


# Auto-Apply
Source: https://docs.gitar.ai/features/code-review/auto-apply

Let Gitar push fixes for review findings, limited by criteria you write

## Overview

Auto-apply lets Gitar commit fixes for review findings and CI failures to a PR's branch. Organization and repository criteria limit which review findings Gitar may fix.

Turn auto-apply on for a PR with [`gitar auto-apply:on`](/commands#auto-apply) or the [`gitar-managed`](/labels#gitar-managed) label. Saving criteria does not turn auto-apply on for any PR.

## Set Organization Criteria

Write criteria in **Settings -> Configuration** to limit automatic review fixes across the organization.

Auto-apply review criteria card with the organization review criteria field and a note on extending it with .gitar/config/apply.md

1. Open **Settings -> Configuration** in the Gitar dashboard.
2. Find the **Auto-apply review criteria** card.
3. Enter criteria under **Organization review criteria**.
4. Select **Save**.

Criteria take effect on each PR's next run. Use natural language to filter by finding severity, issue type, affected code, or [PR risk](/features/code-review/risk-triage). Examples:

* "Only fix unused imports and formatting. Skip payment logic."
* "Only fix minor findings on low-risk PRs."
* "Skip findings in authentication or authorization code."

> [!NOTE]
>
> The fixer cannot run shell commands or tests, so criteria that require passing tests are never met. An unavailable risk assessment does not meet a risk-dependent condition.

## Per-Repo Criteria

Add `.gitar/config/apply.md` to extend organization criteria for one repository. When both are present, a fix must satisfy both. Without the file, organization criteria apply on their own.

Gitar reads the file from the default branch, so changes inside a PR cannot authorize that PR's own fixes. See [Repository Customization](/configuration/repository-config) for the `.gitar` directory.

```bash theme={null}
mkdir -p .gitar/config
echo "Skip findings in database migrations." > .gitar/config/apply.md
```

## What Criteria Control

Criteria select fixes for code review findings only. CI fixes, [CI recovery](/features/ci-failure-analysis#recovery-after-a-gitar-fix), rule actions, and explicit requests such as `gitar fix` work independently of these criteria. Auto-approve and auto-merge have separate criteria.

Each run fixes up to eight findings. Gitar checks every patch against the criteria before committing and again before pushing. A rejected patch is dropped without losing earlier accepted fixes, and its finding stays open.

## Results in the Dashboard Comment

When criteria are set and findings have been evaluated, **Options** in the [dashboard comment](/gitar-dashboard) shows a count such as `Auto-apply: 1 / 3 findings fixed`, with reasons for skipped findings. The count includes only fixes pushed to the branch.


# Auto-Approve
Source: https://docs.gitar.ai/features/code-review/auto-approve

Automatically approve pull requests after a clean code review

## Overview

Auto-approve lets Gitar submit an approving review on your behalf after a clean code review. You define criteria in natural language, and if they are met, Gitar approves the PR without any manual action required.

> [card] Criteria-Driven
>
> Define approval conditions in natural language, or use the default behavior with no criteria at all

> [card] Post-Review Only
>
> Only runs after a clean code review with no blocking findings

> [card] Per-Repo Control
>
> Extend org criteria per repository with a config file

## How It Works

**1. Code review completes**

Gitar reviews the PR as usual. Important or critical findings withhold approval. Approval is also withheld when the configured merge blocking threshold includes unresolved suggestions.

**2. Criteria are checked**

Gitar evaluates your configured criteria against the whole PR, including its changed files and unresolved findings. If no criteria are configured, Gitar approves the PR when the review found no blocking issues.

**3. Approval is submitted**

Gitar submits an approval on the connected code host. The dashboard comment shows the approval and the rationale.

## Enabling Auto-Approve

Auto-approve is configured in your organization settings:

1. Navigate to **Settings -> Configuration** in the Gitar dashboard
2. Open the **Behavior** section
3. Enable the **Auto-approve PRs based on code review** toggle
4. Optionally, enter custom approval criteria in the text area that appears

Criteria are written in natural language. Examples:

* "Only approve if the changes are limited to documentation or test files"
* "Approve changes to the `docs/` directory or files ending in `.md`"
* "Skip approval if the PR modifies authentication or payment logic"

> [!NOTE]
>
> Auto-approval criteria are evaluated against the PR metadata, changed files, and code review
> results, not CI pipeline status. Gitar does not verify conditions like "tests pass" or "linting
> is green". Enforce those with your platform's branch protection or merge checks.
>
> Criteria also cannot call integration tools. Naming a Jira, Slack, or [custom MCP](/integrations/custom-integrations)
> tool in your criteria does not make its result an approval condition, and results from earlier
> integration calls are not passed into the evaluation.

If you change auto-approve or merge blocking settings after a review, Gitar requires a fresh review before submitting a pending automatic approval. Turning off auto-approve prevents pending automatic approvals from being submitted. Actions already sent to the code host may still complete.

### Use PR risk in criteria

Approval criteria can use Gitar's whole-PR risk assessment, for example, "Approve only low- or medium-risk changes." [Risk Triage](/features/code-review/risk-triage) explains the levels and team-defined policy.

Risk restricts approval after review. Low risk does not bypass blocking findings, and an unavailable assessment does not meet a risk-dependent condition unless the criteria explicitly allow it.

### Require code owner or human approval

On GitHub, criteria can use `CODEOWNERS` and human reviews. For example:

```text theme={null}
Only approve when a code owner other than the author has approved the current head.
```

Gitar reads `CODEOWNERS` from the PR's target branch. It checks who owns the changed files and whether each review applies to the current commit. Team ownership requires the GitHub App's organization **Members: read** permission.

If ownership or review evidence cannot be read, the condition is unmet and Gitar withholds approval. These criteria are unavailable on other code hosts, where they also withhold approval.

If the review history is too large to evaluate completely, Gitar withholds criteria-based approval and auto-merge for that run.

### Default Behavior (No Criteria)

With the toggle enabled and no criteria in organization settings, configuration repositories, or `.gitar/config/approve.md`, Gitar approves PRs with no blocking findings. Minor suggestions may remain unless the configured merge blocking threshold includes them.

## After Approval

When auto-approve fires, you can optionally chain additional actions from the same settings section.

### `gitar-approved` Label

Enable **Apply gitar-approved label** to mark approved PRs. See [`gitar-approved`](/labels#gitar-approved) for label behavior.

### Auto-Merge

Enable **Auto-merge PRs** to merge automatically after approval. See [Auto-Merge](/features/code-review/auto-merge) for platform prerequisites and merge methods.

## Per-Repo Criteria

You can define criteria at the repository level by placing a Markdown file at `.gitar/config/approve.md`. Repo criteria work on their own or alongside org-level criteria.

Gitar reads this file from the default branch. Changes take effect after they merge, so a PR cannot change its own approval policy. If Gitar cannot verify the file, it withholds approval for that run.

See [Repository Customization](/configuration/repository-config) for more on the `.gitar` directory and how repo-level config files work.

```bash theme={null}
mkdir -p .gitar/config
echo "Only approve if no database migrations are included." > .gitar/config/approve.md
```

### Combination Logic

Organization criteria include shared approval policy from selected [configuration repositories](/configuration/configuration-repository).

| Org criteria | Repo file | What happens |
| - | - | - |
| Set | Present | Both are combined, so the PR must satisfy all criteria |
| Set | Absent | Org criteria only |
| Empty | Present | Repo file criteria only |
| Empty | Absent | Default: approve unless the review has blocking findings |

## Platform Behavior

Auto-approve works on GitHub, GitLab, Azure DevOps, and both Bitbucket editions. See [Platform Support](/connecting-code/platform-support) for preview availability.

> [accordion] GitHub
>
> Gitar submits an approving review on the PR. If your branch protection rules require an approving review, this satisfies that requirement.
>
> The review body reads: *"Gitar has auto-approved this PR"* with a link to your auto-approve settings.
>
> If auto-merge is also enabled, the review body mentions that auto-merge has been armed.

> [accordion] GitLab
>
> Gitar approves the MR via the API. If your approval rules list Gitar as an eligible approver, this counts toward the required approval count.
>
> If auto-merge is also enabled, Gitar calls the GitLab API to arm auto-merge after approval.

> [accordion] Azure DevOps
>
> Gitar sets an Approved vote on the PR using the connected service account. If auto-merge is enabled, Gitar also enables auto-complete.

> [accordion] Bitbucket
>
> Gitar approves the PR on Cloud and Data Center. Bitbucket Cloud has no auto-merge to arm. On Data Center, Gitar merges the PR itself once Bitbucket's merge checks pass.

## Revocation

Each new review checks the current PR against the current criteria. On GitHub, resolving finding threads can trigger another approval evaluation without a push once the review is eligible. The dashboard shows **Re-evaluating** while that check runs.

If Gitar integrates other commits while pushing a fix, it requires a fresh review before auto-approving the updated branch.

If a new push introduces blocking findings, Gitar revokes its previous approval:

* On GitHub, Gitar dismisses its approving review.
* On GitLab, Gitar calls the unapprove API.
* On Azure DevOps, Gitar clears its approval vote.
* On Bitbucket, Gitar removes its approval.
* Gitar also attempts to [cancel auto-merge it enabled](/features/code-review/auto-merge#when-approval-is-revoked).

If the `gitar-approved` label was applied, Gitar removes it when the approval is revoked.

> [!NOTE]
>
> If Gitar cannot complete approval evaluation, it skips approval for that run.


# Auto-Merge
Source: https://docs.gitar.ai/features/code-review/auto-merge

Automatically merge pull requests after Gitar approves them

## Overview

Auto-merge works alongside [Auto-Approve](/features/code-review/auto-approve). When Gitar approves a PR, it also enables your platform's native auto-merge so the PR merges as soon as all other conditions are met, such as passing CI and required approvals.

Not every host has a native auto-merge to arm. See [Platform Support](/connecting-code/platform-support).

> [card] Hands-Free Merging
>
> Approved PRs merge automatically once all checks pass

> [card] Platform-Native
>
> Uses your platform's built-in auto-merge, not a custom queue

> [card] Safe by Default
>
> Gitar attempts to cancel auto-merge it enabled when approval is revoked.

## How It Works

**1. Gitar approves the PR**

Auto-approve runs after a clean code review. If the PR meets your configured criteria, Gitar submits an approving review.

**2. Auto-merge is enabled**

Immediately after approving, Gitar enables auto-merge on the PR using your platform's native feature.

**3. The platform merges when ready**

The PR merges once CI passes and required approvals and branch protection rules are satisfied. If every requirement is already satisfied, the reviewed change can merge immediately.

## Prerequisites

Before enabling auto-merge in Gitar, make sure your platform is configured to allow it.

> [accordion] GitHub
>
> Enable **Allow auto-merge** in your repository settings under **General**. Without it, Gitar approves the PR but auto-merge is not armed.

> [accordion] GitLab
>
> No additional configuration is needed. GitLab uses the project's default merge settings.
>
> Pushing new commits to a GitLab MR cancels the armed auto-merge. If Gitar's next review also approves the MR, auto-merge is re-enabled automatically.

> [accordion] Azure DevOps
>
> Gitar enables Azure DevOps auto-complete using the connected service account. Azure DevOps completes the PR when its merge policies are satisfied, including immediately if it is already ready to merge.

## Enabling Auto-Merge

Auto-merge is configured alongside auto-approve in your organization settings:

1. Navigate to **Settings -> Configuration** in the Gitar dashboard
2. Open the **Behavior** section
3. Make sure **Auto-approve** is enabled first
4. Enable the **Auto-merge PRs** toggle
5. On GitHub or Azure DevOps, select the merge method: **Merge commit**, **Squash**, or **Rebase**

If you change auto-approve, merge blocking, or auto-merge settings after a review, Gitar requires a fresh review before enabling auto-merge. Turning off auto-merge for a PR prevents pending requests from enabling it. Actions already sent to the code host may still complete.

## Auto-Merge Criteria

By default every auto-approved PR is armed for merge. Criteria narrow that down.

Write them in plain language in **Settings -> Configuration -> Behavior -> Auto-merge criteria**. Gitar evaluates them against the PR's metadata, changed files, and diff size, separately from the auto-approve criteria.

On GitHub, merge criteria can also use [code owner and human approval evidence](/features/code-review/auto-approve#require-code-owner-or-human-approval). If required evidence is unavailable, Gitar does not arm auto-merge.

A PR can be approved and still not be armed.

```text theme={null}
Only arm auto-merge when the change is confined to docs/ or the dependency lockfile.
```

Criteria cannot reference CI status. Your platform already holds the merge until its own checks pass, so a criterion like "tests are green" is treated as satisfied.

### Use PR risk in criteria

Merge criteria can use the same [risk assessment](/features/code-review/risk-triage) as code review and auto-approve, for example, "Merge only low-risk changes." A clean review does not by itself establish low risk.

### Per-Repository Criteria

A repository can add its own criteria by committing `.gitar/config/merge.md`:

```markdown .gitar/config/merge.md theme={null}
Only arm auto-merge for changes under packages/ui.
Never arm auto-merge on a PR that touches a migration.
```

Repository criteria are **added** to your organization's, including shared merge policy from [configuration repositories](/configuration/configuration-repository). A PR has to satisfy all criteria.

Gitar reads the file from your default branch, so a PR that adds or deletes it cannot change its own outcome.

### Requiring the File

Turn on **Require `.gitar/config/merge.md`** to make auto-merge opt-in per repository. With it on, Gitar arms auto-merge only in repositories that have committed the file. Everywhere else the approval still lands and the merge is left to you.

This is the setting to use when you want auto-merge available org-wide but each team deciding for itself.

> [!NOTE]
>
> If the file exists but Gitar cannot read it, auto-merge is held for that run. The approval still goes through, and the next push re-evaluates.

### Overriding on a Single PR

Comment `gitar auto-merge:on` to enable auto-merge for that PR regardless of merge criteria, or `gitar auto-merge:off` to disable it. Gitar must still approve the PR, and platform merge requirements still apply.

## What Happens If It Fails

If Gitar cannot enable auto-merge, it posts a message in the dashboard comment explaining why. The PR remains approved. Common reasons include:

| Reason | What to do |
| - | - |
| Auto-merge is not allowed for this repository | Enable "Allow auto-merge" in your GitHub repo settings |
| Gitar lacks merge permission | Grant Gitar the necessary permissions in your repository |
| The PR is a draft or has unresolved threads | Mark the PR as ready and resolve open threads |

## When Approval is Revoked

If a new push introduces blocking findings, Gitar revokes its approval and attempts to cancel auto-merge that it enabled. Auto-merge or auto-complete enabled by another user or integration is left unchanged on GitHub, GitLab, and Azure DevOps.

> [!NOTE]
>
> Auto-merge requires [Auto-Approve](/features/code-review/auto-approve) to be enabled. Gitar only arms auto-merge on PRs it has approved.


# Block Merge
Source: https://docs.gitar.ai/features/code-review/block-merge

Prevent pull requests from merging with unresolved findings

## Overview

Gitar can prevent a PR from being merged when its code review finds issues above a severity threshold you configure. The block uses your platform's native review mechanism, requesting changes on GitHub or unapproving on GitLab, so it works with your existing branch protection rules.

## How It Works

**1. Code review completes**

Gitar reviews the PR as usual, producing a verdict based on the findings.

**2. Verdict is evaluated against the threshold**

If the verdict meets or exceeds your configured blocking level, Gitar submits a blocking review on the PR.

**3. Issues are resolved**

As you push fixes and findings are resolved, Gitar re-evaluates the verdict. Once it drops below the threshold, Gitar dismisses its blocking review and the PR can merge.

Gitar blocking a PR merge due to unresolved code review findings

## Enabling Block Merge

Go to **Settings -> Configuration** and find the **Behavior** card. Set **Block merges** to the lowest severity that should hold a PR:

| Level | Blocks when the review finds |
| - | - |
| **Never** | Nothing. Block Merge is off |
| **Critical issues only** | Critical findings |
| **Important or higher** | Important or critical findings. This is the default level |
| **Suggestions or higher** | Any finding, including suggestions |

Select **Customize** on a repository to set a different level for it.

## Platform Behavior

Whether a block is enforced or only recorded differs by host. See [Platform Support](/connecting-code/platform-support) for the full matrix.

> [accordion] GitHub
>
> Gitar submits a "request changes" review on the PR. If your branch protection rules require approving reviews or no outstanding change requests, this blocks the merge button.
>
> When the block is lifted, Gitar dismisses its review. Repository admins can also dismiss the blocking review manually through GitHub's UI. Gitar respects that override and does not re-block for the current review cycle.

> [accordion] GitLab
>
> Gitar adds an MR-level approval rule named **Gitar**, adds itself as a reviewer, and comments that the MR is blocked. The rule holds the merge until Gitar approves.
>
> Approval rules have to be enforced on the instance for this to hold a merge. Where they are not, Gitar places no rule and posts no blocking comment - the findings stay in the review summary and the MR can merge.
>
> When issues are resolved, Gitar approves the MR to unblock it.

> [accordion] Azure DevOps
>
> Gitar casts a reject vote on the PR. Azure DevOps treats that as a hard block and refuses to complete the PR while it stands, with no branch policy needed.

> [accordion] Bitbucket
>
> On Cloud and Data Center the review is recorded and visible, and Bitbucket does not treat it as a merge gate, so a person can still merge.

> [accordion] AWS CodeCommit preview
>
> Gitar adds a native approval rule that holds the merge while blocking findings remain. When the review clears or an authorized user runs `gitar unblock`, Gitar removes its own rule. Existing customer approval rules remain in place.

## Overriding a Block

If you need to merge despite unresolved findings, you have two options:

* **[`gitar unblock`](/commands#unblock) command**: comment `gitar unblock` on the PR to dismiss the blocking review and allow merging. An admin turns the command on with **Enable the `gitar unblock` command** in the Behavior card.
* **Platform override**: dismiss the blocking review through your host's own UI.


# Risk Triage
Source: https://docs.gitar.ai/features/code-review/risk-triage

Classify whole-PR risk and apply team policy to review, approval, and merge decisions

Gitar classifies each PR as Low, Medium, or High risk to help reviewers decide where to focus attention. The assessment reflects the impact of the whole change, including sensitive behavior that a small diff can affect.

## What the risk levels mean

Risk describes the change's potential impact. It is separate from the severity of an individual finding and the overall review verdict.

| Level | Default meaning | Example |
| - | - | - |
| Low | Narrow changes with little behavior or interface impact. | A documentation correction. |
| Medium | Bounded behavior changes, substantial internal changes, or uncertain impact. | A localized bug fix outside sensitive behavior. |
| High | Sensitive behavior or broad impact. | Authorization changes, payment calculations, or schema migrations. |

A PR can have no reported defects and still be high risk. For example, a correct change to payment calculations may need closer human review because of the behavior it affects.

## Where risk appears

The risk label appears inside the expanded **Code Review** section of the PR dashboard comment. The Gitar dashboard also shows risk on recent activity rows and a risk breakdown on Overview when assessments are available.

Example expanded Code Review with a risk assessment and review verdict

Gitar hides the previous label while a review is processing. Older reviews without an assessment gain one when another review runs.

## How Gitar assesses risk

Gitar uses the whole PR diff and file-change information, so a small follow-up push does not hide the risk of earlier changes in the same PR.

**1. Assess the whole change**

Gitar applies the default definitions and the organization's risk instructions to the PR.

**2. Apply repository guidance**

Repository instructions can raise the risk level to reflect local constraints. They cannot lower the organization's assessment.

**3. Refresh before automated decisions**

When the change needs reassessment, including after a Gitar fix, Gitar refreshes risk before evaluating approval or merge criteria.

Uncertain impact normally produces Medium risk. If Gitar cannot complete the assessment, it uses High risk so a failed assessment cannot satisfy a low-risk approval condition.

## Define risk for the organization

Organization admins set policy under **Settings -> Configuration -> Custom Instructions**. **Risk classification** turns assessment on or off, and **Risk instructions** refines what each level means for the team.

Risk classification is on by default. Leaving the instructions empty uses Gitar's default definitions.

For example:

```text theme={null}
Changes to payment calculations or authorization decisions are high risk.
Documentation-only changes are low risk.
```

Turning classification off hides risk labels and retains saved instructions. Risk-dependent approval or merge criteria require an assessment unless those criteria explicitly allow unavailable risk.

## Add repository guidance

The file `.gitar/config/risk.md` adds risk instructions for one repository.

```markdown theme={null}
Changes to the settlement schedule are high risk.
Changes to exported client interfaces require at least medium risk.
```

| Organization assessment | Repository guidance | Final level |
| - | - | - |
| Low | The changed subsystem is high risk. | High |
| Medium | The change is low risk. | Medium |
| High | No additional guidance. | High |

Gitar considers both the default-branch policy and additions in the PR. Deleting or emptying the PR's copy does not remove the default-branch policy from that review.

Policy changes apply when a review runs. Saving instructions does not reclassify every historical PR.

## Use risk in approval and merge criteria

Risk can restrict existing automation after code review. Low risk alone does not approve or merge a PR.

| Setting | Example criterion |
| - | - |
| [Auto-approve](/features/code-review/auto-approve) | Approve only low- or medium-risk changes. |
| [Auto-merge](/features/code-review/auto-merge) | Merge only low-risk changes. |

Blocking findings and platform checks still apply. The same assessment feeds these decisions, so the approval step does not invent a different risk level from a clean verdict.

## Related

> [card] [Code Review](/features/code-review)
>
> Findings, verdicts, and review coverage.

> [card] [Functional Validation](/features/functional-validation)
>
> Track whether the change implements the linked issue's requirements.


# Cross-PR Analysis
Source: https://docs.gitar.ai/features/cross-pr-analysis

Catch changes that could break logic another PR relies on

> [!NOTE]
>
> Cross-PR Analysis is available on the **Enterprise** plan.

## Overview

Gitar compares a PR with related open PRs to spot changes that could break behavior another change depends on. Findings help teams decide which side needs adjusting before the changes land.

Two PRs can affect the same logic without editing the same lines. One might remove an API field while another adds a caller that still expects it.

## What it catches

| One PR changes | Another PR depends on |
| - | - |
| An API field or response format. | A caller reading the previous format. |
| An event name or payload. | A handler expecting the previous event. |
| A shared function's behavior. | A new caller relying on its earlier behavior. |
| A database column or its meaning. | A query or job using the previous schema. |

These checks concern how changes work together. A clean merge can still introduce a behavior mismatch.

## How it works

**1. Find related open changes**

During a review, Gitar identifies open PRs whose changes may interact with the PR under review, in the same repository or another connected repository.

**2. Check the shared behavior**

Gitar compares the relevant code to identify a mismatch and the conditions under which it would matter.

**3. Report what needs adjusting**

Findings appear inline with a **cross-pr** label and a link to the related PR. The explanation describes the interaction and can suggest changes on either side.

Teams can use the finding to coordinate a compatible change or decide the order in which the PRs should land. Findings follow the same [feedback and resolution flow](/features/code-review#providing-feedback) as other code review findings.

## Inspect Cross-PR findings

Enterprise organization members and admins can open **Dashboard -> Cross-PR** to browse recorded findings across the organization.

Cross-PR dashboard with totals for open findings, PRs involved, and resolved findings above the findings list

Admins use the Cross-PR toggle on that page to turn the feature on or off. Turning it off stops future processing, and recorded findings stay readable.

On GitLab, the dashboard is labeled **Cross-MR** and links to related merge requests.

| Summary | What it shows |
| - | - |
| Open findings. | Findings open within the selected repository and time range. |
| PRs involved. | Distinct PRs on either side of the recorded findings in that scope. |
| Resolved findings. | Findings resolved within that scope. |

Filter by repository and time range to scope the summary. Status filters and text search narrow the findings list.

Select a finding to inspect the explanation and the related PR. Expand **Code evidence and revisions** for the recorded file references and compared revisions, when available.

The detail view includes suggested adjustments on each side when the review recorded them. Links lead back to the PRs so the teams can coordinate the next change.

## Reading recorded comparisons

Findings describe the revisions compared at the recorded time. They are not a current merge verdict, and changes to the related PR do not automatically refresh an existing finding.

Gitar does not publish a new finding if the related PR has closed, merged, or changed since the comparison. Previously recorded findings remain available in the dashboard.

## Related

> [card] [Cross-Repo Analysis](/features/cross-repo-analysis)
>
> Check changes against shared contracts in other repositories.

> [card] [Knowledge](/features/knowledge)
>
> Bring team conventions and lessons from earlier reviews into the review.

> [card] [Code Review](/features/code-review)
>
> How findings are posted and resolved.


# Cross-Repo Analysis
Source: https://docs.gitar.ai/features/cross-repo-analysis

Catch changes that break another repository in your organization

> [!NOTE]
>
> Cross-Repo Analysis is available on the **Enterprise** plan.

## Overview

Gitar checks your pull request against other repositories in your organization and reports what the change breaks in them.

Repositories are related by more than imports. A route path, an event name, or a database column that two repositories have to agree on ties them together, with no shared package between them.

> [card] Automatic scanning
>
